**Owns:** the context snapshot for anyone, human or agent, picking up Memoshi.
**Depends on:** [`plan/MASTER_PLAN.md`](plan/MASTER_PLAN.md), [`plan/decisions.md`](plan/decisions.md), [`plan/AMENDMENT_2026-11-13.md`](plan/AMENDMENT_2026-11-13.md)

This file replaces the earlier handoff that pointed at `~/Documents/Memoshi-Plan`. That handoff was checked on 2026-10-20, and its corrections are listed in [§5](#5-corrections-to-the-previous-handoff). **This repo is now the single source of truth** ([D36](plan/decisions.md#d36), [D65](plan/decisions.md#d65)). Code started on 2026-10-23 with the native app shells, protocol and CI. The rest of Phase 0 starts on Monday 2026-10-26, and its gate is Fri 2026-11-27. Every later date is an estimate ([D122](plan/decisions.md#d122), which supersedes [D87](plan/decisions.md#d87)'s calendar): milestone M1 closes Phase 1 on Fri 2027-10-29, M2 closes Phase 2 on Fri 2029-06-29, and M3, the v1.0 gate, closes Phase 4 on Fri 2030-09-27; two feature drops follow, with gates on Fri 2030-12-06 and Fri 2031-02-21, and v1.1 starts on Mon 2031-02-24. v1 is for the user's own Mac, iPhone and iPad, with no App Store ([D67](plan/decisions.md#d67)).

---

## 1. Read in this order

1. [`plan/MASTER_PLAN.md`](plan/MASTER_PLAN.md): the product, vocabulary, object model, architecture, scope and roadmap at a glance. The vocabulary is binding. It is revised through D122: [§11](plan/MASTER_PLAN.md#11-scope) carries v1.0's scope by phase and [§12](plan/MASTER_PLAN.md#12-roadmap-at-a-glance) the stages and gates.
2. [`plan/decisions.md`](plan/decisions.md): decisions D1–D139, open decisions O1–O13, the mapping from Draft 3, and change control. Then [`plan/AMENDMENT_2026-11-13.md`](plan/AMENDMENT_2026-11-13.md): the summary of record of what D106–D122 changed and what v1.0 contains after them, by milestone. The plan documents and specs were revised to match on 2026-11-13; where one still disagrees, the decisions win, then the amendment.
3. [`plan/feature-catalog.md`](plan/feature-catalog.md) and [`plan/10-roadmap.md`](plan/10-roadmap.md), both revised for D106–D122: every feature with its phase and priority, and the phases, the estimated calendar and the cut order. Then the plan doc for your area ([§6](#6-where-everything-lives)) and the matching spec in [`specs/`](specs/).
4. [`plan/phase-0.md`](plan/phase-0.md) before writing any code.
5. For implementation, review, debugging, testing, localization, reference-app or agent-guidance work, use the [development-skill routing table](skills.md#routing); load the matching entrypoint and relevant references only. The catalogue records source locations, prerequisites, license gates and known corrections, not enabled tools or new product decisions.

Conventions in every doc: `[INFERENCE]` marks reasoning rather than a sourced fact, "(vendor claim)" marks an unverified maker statement, and `Dn` links to the decision log. To change a decision, add a new one that supersedes it; never edit one silently.

### Scope amendment of 2026-11-13

The user compared Memoshi with Cognitio, `bot-hq`, Pi's `p2` and Ultimate ([merge assessment](research/2026-11-12-memoshi-cognitio-bot-hq-merge.md)) and decided D106–D117 one by one. D118–D122 followed the same day: they settle the mail class, the word App, the default providers, the policy classes for the new tools and the estimated calendar. Every plan document and spec was revised on 2026-11-13 to match, through the roadmap, spec and design reviews that the [amendment §7](plan/AMENDMENT_2026-11-13.md#7-reviews-these-decisions-trigger) lists. This block is therefore a summary of what changed, not a list of pending planning work. The [amendment](plan/AMENDMENT_2026-11-13.md) has the scope by area, and its [§8](plan/AMENDMENT_2026-11-13.md#8-open-items) lists what is still undecided.

Phases keep their numbers. M1, M2 and M3 name the gates at the ends of Phases 1, 2 and 4, and v1.0 is M3 passed. Where D106–D117 say "in M1", "in M2" or "in M3", read Phase 1, Phase 2 and Phase 3 ([D122](plan/decisions.md#d122)).

- [D106](plan/decisions.md#d106): Cognitio's design leads the shell on Mac, iPad and iPhone, rebuilt in Memoshi's own code as one SwiftUI codebase, with no Kuvo- or Cognitio-derived source file, asset or string. Viticci's grammar stays for native Apple-data cards, Apps and phone navigation. The earlier preview is frozen at git tag `design-preview-2026-11-13`.
- [D107](plan/decisions.md#d107): Cognitio keeps developing in parallel in its own repository; it is the behaviour reference and the oracle for ports. Memoshi's agents do not edit it.
- [D108](plan/decisions.md#d108): Claude through Claude Code, as the model of any bot on the Mac (Phase 1) and as a coding worker (Phase 2); no Anthropic sign-in inside the app.
- [D109](plan/decisions.md#d109): two ways of working, both first-class: through the Assistant, which does small things itself and hands the rest off through Handoffs, or driving bots and sessions directly. Bot-to-bot messages in Phase 1; Groups and Goals in Phase 2.
- [D110](plan/decisions.md#d110): external coding agents (Codex app-server, Claude Code, ACP) in Phase 2, on the Mac only; an optional team mode per project, with a landing gate that is a program, not a model; self-checks from Phase 1.
- [D111](plan/decisions.md#d111): voice with a cloud realtime engine and an on-device engine; the Mac in Phase 2, iPhone and iPad in Phase 3.
- [D112](plan/decisions.md#d112): `web_search`, `x_search` and a built-in connector catalog with connect cards in Phase 1; connector event triggers, optional Composio, and image and video generation in Phase 2.
- [D113](plan/decisions.md#d113): a full computer per bot, chosen per bot: This Mac (sandboxed), a Bot computer (a local VM, at the end of Phase 2) or a Cloud computer (Phase 2, after the local ones). Remote machines, the always-on Mac host, and watching and taking over a bot's browser or computer from iPhone and iPad come in Phase 3.
- [D114](plan/decisions.md#d114): Apps that bots build and extend: a pane beside the chat, exported functions, the `view.*` tools and selection carry. Page Apps in Phase 1; Swift Apps in Phase 2 on the Mac and Phase 3 on iPhone and iPad. A Mac-only plugin developer mode, off by default, with an opt-in Full access on this Mac per App or plugin.
- [D115](plan/decisions.md#d115): generated interface in three layers: streamed catalog cards (Phase 1), bot-written Swift, and sandboxed web pages; native diagrams, charts and math.
- [D116](plan/decisions.md#d116): `mail.*` tools for Gmail, Microsoft 365 and IMAP, and built-in panes for Mail, Calendar, Reminders merged with Tasks, Contacts, Notes, and Files with the Library; the Mac in Phase 1, iPhone and iPad in Phase 3.
- [D117](plan/decisions.md#d117): milestones M1–M3 replace the single v1.0 gate; nothing leaves v1.0.
- [D118](plan/decisions.md#d118): tidying a mailbox (archive, mark read or unread, label, move, star) is its own lighter class, `mail.organize`, which a standing rule can cover. `mail.send` stays T4 and a Draft, except where the user let that bot send mail by itself; deleting, unsubscribing and changing filters stay T5.
- [D119](plan/decisions.md#d119): the word is App, with a capital A, for the built-in ones and the ones bots build; the kinds are page App and Swift App. Identifiers such as the `mini_app` tool keep their names, and "the app" in lower case still means Memoshi.
- [D120](plan/decisions.md#d120): default providers for search, voice, image and video generation and Cloud computers; each is a setting, swappable, on the user's own key.
- [D121](plan/decisions.md#d121): policy classes and tiers for the tools D109–D116 add; [`specs/policy.md`](specs/policy.md) is their normative home.
- [D122](plan/decisions.md#d122): the estimated calendar: M1 on Fri 2027-10-29, M2 on Fri 2029-06-29, the Phase 3 gate on Fri 2030-08-02, and M3, the v1.0 gate, on Fri 2030-09-27, after 197 plan weeks. [§4](#4-plan-at-a-glance) has the table.
- [D123](plan/decisions.md#d123): S2 is rerun on a committed tree with the stricter harness before the gate relies on it; until then revocation at the claim and link levels and the expiry two-writer audit count as unproven. The rerun was filed on 2026-11-13 and passed on `918cb25` ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-02/README.md)). After [D133](plan/decisions.md#d133) it passed again on `4db72e2`, where the authority itself refused a revoked device that had held a lease ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-04/README.md)). It passed on `f22517d`, after [D134](plan/decisions.md#d134) ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-05/README.md)); it passed on `4658dcd` ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-06/README.md)) and on `7df84f9` ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-07/README.md)), each with the checker of its time. The current record is on `a2ac274`, after the 2026-11-18 fixes, with the fail-closed checker and each revocation refusal tied to its probe ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-08/README.md)).
- [D124](plan/decisions.md#d124): the two device holds (a message sealed for an old group state; a reservation that is never released) stay through Phase 0 and get a way out in Phase 1.
- [D125](plan/decisions.md#d125): the Mac app carries the camera entitlement, for the pairing scanner only.
- [D126](plan/decisions.md#d126): one licence rule for every dependency: Rust crates on the same accepted list as Swift packages, with two named exceptions, and the Python packages shipped with Pyodide against a reviewed table.
- [D127](plan/decisions.md#d127): bot names in their avatar colour are corrected to meet the declared contrast everywhere, and the identity badge uses the checked accent pair.
- [D128](plan/decisions.md#d128): before a Chat is compacted, the engine's `beforeCompact` hook runs a memory flush and carries summary instructions.
- [D129](plan/decisions.md#d129): the permission texts for Calendar, Reminders, Contacts and the Phase 2 microphone.
- [D130](plan/decisions.md#d130): smaller points settled after the code review (socket fallback folder, host-call slots, the schema's retry flag, `trash.emptied`, discards, iPhone waiting and enrollment, confirmed values, and the items moved to Phase 1).
- [D131](plan/decisions.md#d131): by the user's instruction the remaining open items are done now: the ways out of the two device holds, the five items D130 had moved to Phase 1 and the host-call deadline rule. The canonical CI run and the S2 rerun follow on that commit.
- [D132](plan/decisions.md#d132): a pairing message may span several encrypted messages (at most 256 KiB), and a device group holds at most 64 devices. The full S2 run found that the 45th device could not pair.
- [D133](plan/decisions.md#d133): device groups are qualified at 8 devices (about 54 stays a stress test); group state is saved as a journal; the limits reported after the 2026-11-13 runs are closed where this Mac allows.
- [D134](plan/decisions.md#d134) (2026-11-14): Memoshi's Pi_Swift pin moves from the development candidate `892585fc` to the tag `slice-2` (commit `ce1728ef8236d95a8b94b50a856670c48ad85365`, tagged 2026-11-12). From this pin on, S1 results count for the gate ([D90](plan/decisions.md#d90)); results recorded on the candidate stay development evidence. [Pin move](#d134-pin-move-2026-11-14) has the details.
- [D135](plan/decisions.md#d135) (2026-11-14, S3): the new-tab (`target=_blank`) preconnect, one empty connection outside the gateway, is a named exception in the S3 report and the gate review, not a pass. Memoshi reports it to Apple and WebKit and builds no network filter now; the gap closes when bots browse only in their own Bot computers.
- [D136](plan/decisions.md#d136) (2026-11-14, S3): a signed-in bot that opens another site does so in a separate, throwaway profile with no cookies or storage, under the same gateway, deny-local rules and Lockdown. It replaces the refusal of every cross-site move; built and committed (`fabd26e`).
- [D137](plan/decisions.md#d137) (2026-11-14, S4; amends [D96](plan/decisions.md#d96)): hard links and writes through a file still open after export are accepted limits, not escapes. Before an export Memoshi checks for a process that still has the file open for writing and warns if one does; built and committed (`71111a6`).
- [D138](plan/decisions.md#d138) (2026-11-14; refines [D51](plan/decisions.md#d51)): a step the user stopped offers only Skip and Mark as done, never Run again; an export of a private working copy never offers Run again; other interrupted steps offer all three.

**Reading the older sections.** The dated sections below keep their wording, as records of what was decided and observed at the time. The decisions above replace any line there that places Bot computers, Remote machines, the always-on Mac host, Groups, bot-to-bot messages, external coding agents, Attempts, GUI computer use, Cloud computers or voice in v1.1 or v2; that names D92 or D94 as the design lead; that calls an App a mini app or says it opens only as a sheet on the Mac; or that gives a plan week or a date after Phase 0 from D87's calendar. Phase 0, its tickets and its gate on Fri 2026-11-27 are unchanged.

**Revision of 2026-11-13.** This handoff was revised the same day. The status line, the reading order, this block, the notes on D91, D92 and D94, the research section's authority notes and [§2](#2-the-product-in-five-lines) to [§9](#9-assets-outside-this-repo) now follow D106–D122. No Phase 0 result, proof record or evidence path was changed. The decisions changed no code. A code review of the whole codebase was run on 2026-11-13; its fixes were committed locally the same day, and [Code review and fixes (2026-11-13)](#code-review-2026-11-13) records the result.

**Revision of 2026-11-14 ([D134](plan/decisions.md#d134)–[D138](plan/decisions.md#d138)).** The status line, the reading order and the decision list above add D134 to D138; [§8](#8-next-moves) records the move to the Pi_Swift `slice-2` pin, the owner's S3 and S4 decisions (D135–D137) and the work built that day (committed in `fabd26e` and `71111a6`). Dated sections keep the candidate `892585fc` as the pin of their time.

### Decisions of 2026-10-22

The user decided D44–D87 on 2026-10-22. They remain binding except where later decisions explicitly amend or supersede them, including D88–D93 below and D106–D122 above. D71–D86 come from the review of inferred choices; its [record](plan/inferred-choices-review.md) preserves every reviewed outcome. D87 stages those features: later drops override earlier phase references in the original review, without changing its historical choices. Since 2026-11-13, D117 and D122 re-stage what D106–D116 moved.

- [D44](plan/decisions.md#d44): the engine is Pi_Swift, the Swift port of Pi, always on the latest upstream `main`.
- [D45](plan/decisions.md#d45): one builder directs coding agents, and the calendar is re-planned for one builder ([D122](plan/decisions.md#d122) sets the current estimates; [D87](plan/decisions.md#d87) set the dates before it).
- [D46](plan/decisions.md#d46): Pi_Swift runs in memoshid (a Swift LaunchAgent, no Node) on the Mac and inside the iPhone and iPad app, behind a gate at the Phase 0 review, with fallbacks.
- [D47](plan/decisions.md#d47): v1 ships on Mac, iPhone and iPad; Bot computers, Remote machines and Groups move to v1.1. [D109](plan/decisions.md#d109) and [D113](plan/decisions.md#d113) moved them back into v1.0 on 2026-11-13: Groups and Bot computers in Phase 2, Remote machines in Phase 3.
- [D48](plan/decisions.md#d48): devices link directly, through our relay (self-hostable, plus a hosted instance) and through the user's iCloud, all end-to-end encrypted (to one MLS group since D75).
- [D49](plan/decisions.md#d49): every bot runs on every device: one runner at a time under an epoch-fenced lease (per conversation since D74), live replicas, and tools run where they live. Side-thread answers need an available allowed model; otherwise D88 keeps messages waiting locally.
- [D50](plan/decisions.md#d50): tools are typed, with a shell front end generated from them.
- [D51](plan/decisions.md#d51): an interrupted step runs its outcome check, then shows a Resume card if the answer is not definite.
- [D52](plan/decisions.md#d52): each bot is in Ask first, Auto or Bypass; new bots start in Ask first; Plan and Read-only stop being modes.
- [D53](plan/decisions.md#d53): cards are native for Apple data and decisions, and generated (JSON over about 25 SwiftUI blocks) for the rest.
- [D54](plan/decisions.md#d54): open-source builds may offer Sign in with ChatGPT.
- [D55](plan/decisions.md#d55): formerly required a separate observer, no source access and legal review before the parity spec. [D91](plan/decisions.md#d91) supersedes those restrictions for personal/noncommercial Kuvo code inspection/reuse; private captures still stay outside the repo.
- [D56](plan/decisions.md#d56): Apple platform rules: iOS and iPadOS 27+, the App Store and TestFlight, G2 Developer ID certificates, never Full Disk Access, App Review 2.5.2, 2.5.19 and 4.7. [D67](plan/decisions.md#d67) sets its App Store and App Review rules aside for v1.
- [D57](plan/decisions.md#d57): runs started by outside content (webhooks, connector events, Shortcuts) keep their draft cards even in Bypass; routines, wakes and watches follow one set of rules in Ask first and Auto; Bypass still asks about unknown tools and changes to files that run code outside the sandbox. Closes O12.
- [D58](plan/decisions.md#d58): memoshid ships as an app-shaped helper inside Memoshi.app, with its own provisioning profile, so it uses iCloud (CloudKit) and push itself.
- [D59](plan/decisions.md#d59): an individual Apple Developer Program membership in the user's name, and the permanent ID prefix `com.mehmetcanbudak.memoshi` (iCloud container `iCloud.com.mehmetcanbudak.memoshi`), kept even if the product is renamed.
- [D60](plan/decisions.md#d60): Python 3 runs in each bot's This Mac sandbox (Phase 2), and Pyodide, bundled with NumPy, pandas, SciPy and Matplotlib, runs in the in-app environment on iPhone and iPad (Phase 3), with long runs sent to the Mac; `.ipynb` notebooks open in a native view on every device, and bots read, write and run them. Phase 0 spike S6 checks Pyodide on a mid-range iPhone first (the iPhone 14 Pro since D80); native CPython in the style of Carnets is the fallback (since D79, the last of three: a trimmed, lazily loaded Pyodide, then the Mac's Python, then native CPython).
- [D61](plan/decisions.md#d61): bots may write generated cards as small Swift files, which SwiftSyntax compiles into D53's JSON; the file never runs as code (Phase 2). Superseded by [D68](plan/decisions.md#d68).
- [D62](plan/decisions.md#d62): native mini apps in v1.1: JavaScript logic in Code Mode's engine drives D53's native blocks, on the Mac first and on iPhone and iPad only after App Review is consulted. Superseded by [D68](plan/decisions.md#d68): mini apps are written in Swift and ship in v1.
- [D63](plan/decisions.md#d63): an emulated Linux computer becomes an optional extra on iPhone and iPad in v1.1, on a permissively licensed emulator (Blink or v86; iSH is GPL-3.0 and out); the shell front end stays the default. Superseded by [D69](plan/decisions.md#d69): iSH, in v1.
- [D64](plan/decisions.md#d64): the former WasmKit → `wasm2c` → JavaScriptCore fallback order. **Superseded by [D89](plan/decisions.md#d89)**: `wasm2c` shipping, WasmKit reference/compatibility, no JavaScriptCore.
- [D65](plan/decisions.md#d65): `~/Documents/Memoshi-Plan` continues as a separate prototype with its own agent, on Node and Pi 1.3.0 until Pi_Swift is slice-ready, then on Pi_Swift ([D90](plan/decisions.md#d90) replaced "until the product slice compiles"). It does not bind Memoshi, and Memoshi's agents do not edit it.
- [D66](plan/decisions.md#d66): Phase 2 grows to 17 weeks and Phase 3 to 20, so the v1 gate moves to Fri 2028-02-25, after 66 plan weeks. Its calendar is superseded by [D70](plan/decisions.md#d70).
- [D67](plan/decisions.md#d67): personal use first, with no App Store in v1. The iPhone and iPad app installs on the user's own devices through internal TestFlight (no Beta App Review; each build expires after 90 days, so CI uploads fresh ones) or straight from Xcode; App Review guidelines stop shaping v1, while iOS's own limits still hold; the Mac stays Developer ID and notarized; the friendly alpha becomes optional; a public release is O13. IOS-05 and DIST-08's App Store part are deferred, and R45 with them.
- [D68](plan/decisions.md#d68): one Swift engine for live cards and mini apps. Bots write SwiftUI-style Swift; SwiftSyntax parses it, Memoshi's compiler emits bytecode, Memoshi's interpreter runs it with no JIT, and native SwiftUI components draw it. Live cards sit inline in conversations; mini apps have versions, saved data and an "Ask the bot to change it" box. D85 makes the Mac default a sheet with Open in Window and Full Screen; iPhone and iPad retain full screen. Code reaches data only through Memoshi's tools, under policy and time and memory limits; unsupported code fails at compile time with a fixable error. JSON cards (D53, now with a `tabs` block) and web mini apps stay. The core ships on Mac in Phase 2 and iPhone/iPad in Phase 3; D87 puts jobs, widgets and the signed bytecode cache in Drop 1.
- [D69](plan/decisions.md#d69): an optional emulated Linux computer on iPhone and iPad through iSH (Open Minis's ARM64 version) with Alpine: a package manager, a real shell, Python, Git, SSH and ffmpeg, where Memoshi's app commands are real programs (Phase 3). iSH lives in its own GPL-3.0 module that only the iPhone and iPad app links, so that app is a GPL-3.0 combined work; the Mac app links no GPL code.
- [D70](plan/decisions.md#d70): Phase 2 grows to 23 weeks (W24–W46) and Phase 3 to 24 (W47–W70), and Phase 4 shrinks to 5 (W71–W75), so the v1 gate moves to Fri 2028-04-28, after 75 plan weeks; Phase 5 starts Mon 2028-05-01. Its calendar is superseded by [D87](plan/decisions.md#d87).
- [D71](plan/decisions.md#d71): the Swift engine's rules, amending D68. A mini app may declare background refresh jobs, on a schedule or when data it uses changes, which the bot's runner runs without a screen in a sandboxed engine helper (on the Mac a helper process with no secrets, never memoshid; on iPhone and iPad inside the app, when iOS grants background time), and it may offer home-screen widgets on Mac, iPhone and iPad, drawn from its saved data (CH-26, CH-27). The compiler lists every tool and web host a version can call; the user approves the list when a live card or mini app first appears and again when a new version adds to it, with risky abilities named plainly, and the runner refuses any call outside it. Within its list a program may call any tool its bot can call, under the bot's mode, grants and policy. Source and saved data have no fixed size limits, and a mini app may span several files. The runner compiles and signs bytecode that syncs with the source as a cache; another device runs it only if a paired device signed it, the engine version matches and its own verifier accepts it. About 5.5 weeks [INFERENCE], tallied for one calendar change at the end of the review.
- [D72](plan/decisions.md#d72): the Swift engine's language, look, data migrations and untrusted content, amending D68 and D71. Programs may use nearly all of Swift: classes (`@Observable` and `ObservableObject` included), protocols with extensions, their own generics, `async let`, task groups and actors; unsafe pointers, `@_silgen_name`, dynamic loading, Objective-C interop, `#if` and macros other than `#Preview` and `@Observable` stay out, and data still comes only through tools. Mini apps may style richly (shadows, materials, overlays, Canvas drawing, images from the bot's files), with contrast checked at compile time and Dynamic Type and Reduce Motion honoured; live cards stay semantic-only. Publishing compares a version's saved types with the stored data: renamed or removed fields need a migration the bot writes, or the publish fails with a fix. Every value records where it came from, and policy's exposure effects follow the values that reach a call's arguments, in the style of CaMeL. A version written after untrusted content runs only after the user approves it, seeing what changed and its permission list, and its T4 actions keep draft cards even in Bypass. Medium and low inferred choices are reviewed as area tables. About 9.5 weeks [INFERENCE]; the review's tally is about 15 weeks so far, for one calendar change at its end.
- [D73](plan/decisions.md#d73): multi-device correctness, amending D49 (its "runner follows the user" bullet is superseded) and D71. Bot settings, routines and skills can be edited on any device, offline included, and merge field by field; restrictive changes win (a revoked grant, a lowered mode, a paused routine or a stop beats a concurrent loosening), while card answers, approvals and drafts still go to the runner and histories keep one writer. A response made while a card's runner is unreachable expires with that card. An awake, reachable runner keeps its conversation: other devices forward messages to it and stream its answer; the lease moves when the runner is unreachable or through D74's idle rebalancing. Mini app saved data is a CRDT that merges per item and per field. A runner whose lease the authority has not confirmed is provisional: it drafts but makes no external effect. Every external effect's intent is first copied to another device or the relay, so a runner that takes over runs the outcome check instead of repeating the effect. About 4.25 weeks [INFERENCE]; the review's tally is about 19.25 weeks so far.
- [D74](plan/decisions.md#d74): leases per conversation, rebalancing, handoffs, versions, Trash and a local-model fallback, amending D49 and D73. Each conversation (a Chat, a Session, a lane) has its own store and lease, so one bot can be active on two devices at once; bot-wide records merge, and bot-wide limits use merged counters that can briefly overshoot [INFERENCE] (budgets use slices since D76). An idle conversation's lease returns to an awake, reachable Mac. Handoff work runs under the receiving bot's destination-conversation lease and store, and its result joins the sender's conversation. Store migrations expand, then contract, so the previous app version still runs; anything older is a read-only replica with **Update Memoshi**. Deleted conversations and bots stay in Trash for 30 days on every device, and Empty Trash purges them everywhere (DEV-12). D80 supersedes the original Apple-model default with an open phone/tablet model; D87 stages MOD-09 in Drop 2, and D88 defines waiting when no allowed model is available. About 3.25 weeks [INFERENCE]; the review's tally is about 22.5 weeks so far.
- [D75](plan/decisions.md#d75): security across devices, amending D48 (encryption) and D49. Model keys, OAuth tokens and other secrets are synchronizable Keychain items that iCloud Keychain syncs end to end encrypted, and any secret can be marked "this device only"; every secret stays readable after first unlock, so unattended runs work while the Mac is locked. Sends, money and credential approvals (T4 and T5) need Face ID, Touch ID or the passcode on every device. Stop All Bots stays global and wins over concurrent offline edits. The user's devices form one MLS group (RFC 9420): everything that crosses devices, the iCloud sync log included, is encrypted to it, with forward secrecy and a new epoch when a device joins or is revoked; pairing joins the group through the QR code, and revoking erases a device's replicas by withdrawing its keys. Each bot's browser cookies sync under the group, so a bot signed in on one device is signed in on all; cookie sync ships in Drop 2 under D87, not Phase 3. The original review estimated about 4 weeks [INFERENCE], taking its tally to about 26.5 weeks.
- [D76](plan/decisions.md#d76): budget slices, bot isolation on iPhone and iPad, and Python's runtimes, network and rules, amending D60 and D74. Each active runner reserves a slice of the bot's remaining budget through the lease authority, spends within it and returns the rest when its conversation goes idle or its lease ends, so a bot never overruns its cap by more than one in-flight model call per runner (BU-03, BU-07). On iPhone and iPad each bot works through handles to its own and its linked folders, resolved without following symlinks, and secret reads go through an in-process broker that logs every release (IOS-03). Each bot gets its own Pyodide, closed when idle or under memory pressure; Python's network on iPhone and iPad goes through the in-app gateway under the bot's allowlists; and "Always allow" for Python covers one script or notebook, so changed code asks again (PY-01, PY-03). About 2.5 weeks [INFERENCE]; the review's tally is about 29 weeks so far.
- [D77](plan/decisions.md#d77): the Mac sandbox, its fallback order, Mac sleep, checkpoints and commit hooks, amending D16. The bot's pi-env daemon stays inside the Seatbelt sandbox that S4 proves, so file tools and commands are both confined; a guard in the daemon resolves paths without following symlinks, and app commands reach memoshid over the daemon's existing channel (CMP-03, CMP-21). If S4 fails, a fixed order follows: S4's other design, chosen by a fixed scorecard, then a separate unprivileged macOS user account with Seatbelt, and a Linux VM only for work that runs on Linux (CMP-02). While runs are active on mains power, Memoshi keeps the Mac from idle sleep by default, though the display may sleep; on battery it does not, and one setting changes both (SH-20, a new row). Each coding turn is a checkpoint: the user can restore its files, its conversation or both, or fork a new session from it (CODE-05). Repository hooks still run inside the session's sandbox, and Memoshi commits outside it with `--no-verify`. About 1 week [INFERENCE]; the review's tally is about 30 weeks so far.
- [D78](plan/decisions.md#d78): Chat continuity, memory checks, mode changes, where programs run and the typed API, amending D46's protocol note, D68 and D71. Memoshi never resets a Chat by itself: compaction and memory keep it going, and after at least 6 hours idle a "Start fresh?" chip offers a reset that only the user makes (CH-14). A T4 or T5 card whose arguments come only from memory says "From memory, not re-checked" and offers Check now, a fresh read of the source (MEM-10). Moving a bot to Auto or Bypass with cards pending asks once; Yes decides them under the new mode, and No leaves them pending (SEC-03). On the Mac every live card and mini app runs in a sandboxed engine helper, one per bot, with no secrets, files or network, so a helper crash stops only that bot's programs; on iPhone and iPad programs run in the app, each in its own interpreter instance (CH-24). Swift types are the protocol's source of truth, with the JSON Schema generated from them, and the iPhone and iPad app calls its in-process engine through the same typed Swift API (SH-02). About 1.5 weeks [INFERENCE]; the review's tally is about 31.5 weeks so far.
- [D79](plan/decisions.md#d79): live Apple-data cards, recovery rules, fallbacks, widget data, backups, Code Mode, the model catalog and gateway sign-ins, amending D53, D60, D64 and D75 and adopting two NEXT_VERSION proposals. Native Apple-data cards become live views in Drop 2 under D87: they show the item's current state, re-read it before a write, show a conflict when it changed elsewhere, and mark a write that timed out "outcome unknown" (AP-10, AP-11). While a step is recovered its whole thread holds; grants are re-checked when a call is dispatched; a failing redactor returns safe output, never the raw text; and nothing retries on its own after the link to a bot's computer was lost (CH-11, SEC-04, SEC-23). If Pyodide fails S6, a trimmed, lazily loaded Pyodide comes first, then phones use the Mac's Python, and native CPython only if both fail (PY-03). On iPhone and iPad bot stores stay private in the app's container; mini app widgets read an App Group snapshot from Drop 1; Shortcuts actions run in the app's own process (CH-27, AP-05, AP-07). Bot stores are left out of device backups, so a restored device re-syncs instead of bringing back stale leases (DEV-07), and a recovery key in iCloud Keychain lets a new device join the MLS group and read the iCloud sync log when no old device is left (DEV-02). Its Code Mode clause is superseded by D89, with no JavaScriptCore backend. Each provider's model list says which models are available, and a signed catalog built daily by CI from upstream Pi supplies prices and limits (MOD-10). OpenRouter's and Radius's sign-ins stay in product builds: they create usage-billed keys and reuse no subscription (MOD-02, DIST-03). The original review estimated about 2.75 weeks [INFERENCE], taking its tally to about 34.25 weeks.
- [D80](plan/decisions.md#d80): reference devices, an open model on the phone, a domain for public files, forks and the only-device backup, superseding D74's default local-model fallback and amending D74's forks and D79's backup rule. The user's iPhone 14 Pro and iPad Pro 12.9-inch (5th generation, M1) are the reference devices for S1, S6, the Swift engine and iSH probes. On iPhone the app requests the increased-memory-limit entitlement; Pyodide, iSH and the on-device model take turns [INFERENCE] (IOS-02). MOD-09 ships in Drop 2 under D87: a downloaded MLX model, sized per device (about 1.7B parameters on iPhone, about 4B on iPad [INFERENCE]), can answer while fully disconnected. Apple's on-device model remains an option where it exists; the iPhone 14 Pro has none. Before MOD-09, or without an available allowed model, D88 retains messages as Waiting for connection. A domain the user owns, served by GitHub Pages or Cloudflare Pages, hosts the MCP client document, universal-link file, Mac update feed, signed model catalog and website; the user picks it by Fri 2026-11-13 for Pi_Swift E29. A forked Session has its own store and lease (CODE-05). The user's only device, with iCloud and relay off, keeps its stores in device backups (DEV-07). The original review estimated about 1.5 weeks [INFERENCE], taking its tally to about 35.75 weeks.
- [D81](plan/decisions.md#d81): review table 1, the Swift engine, card and policy details, amending D43's sidebar parity, D68, D69 and the specs `cards.md` and `policy.md`. Its 62 outcomes, 20 kept as specified and 42 changed, are listed row by row in the [review record](plan/inferred-choices-review.md#table-1). The sidebar gains an **Apps** section for mini apps, a deliberate exception to D43's Kuvo sidebar parity, and Spotlight, Siri and Shortcuts open mini apps (SH-07, AP-05, AP-06). A version the user asked for in the change box switches in by itself; the bot hears a short digest of what the user did in its mini apps at its next turn; `bot.ask` shows an inline confirm sheet with the exact text; a failing handler is switched off, the bot gets one automatic fix attempt per version, and three failures in a minute stop the app (CH-22). The kit adapts by device, and a part a program marks Mac-only shows a lighter fallback on iPhone; charts take about 100,000 points, documents get QuickLook previews, and card text may use bold, italic, code and https links that open in the in-app browser (CH-19, CH-23, CH-24). Each device remembers a card's last tab (CH-25), and bots target the runner's newest version, with an **Update Memoshi** fallback on older devices. On Telegram (v1.1) the owner's messages count as the user only for T0–T1, and T2 and up are confirmed in the app (R11-06). If iSH's traffic cannot be routed through the gateway, it ships with guest networking off; SSH uses a sign-only agent backed by the broker; the engine reaches iSH through a host-side adapter by default (IOS-06). Writes go through a temporary file and a rename (SEC-12); audit chains are copied read-only between devices and merged on export (SEC-20, EXP-07); WebKit's loopback proxy takes a random credential per launch. About 2.5 weeks [INFERENCE]; the review's tally is about 38.25 weeks so far.
- [D82](plan/decisions.md#d82): review table 2, the object model, protocol and events, amending D49, D73, D74 and the specs `object-model.md`, `protocol.md`, `events.md` and `bot-definition.md`; its 49 outcomes are in the [review record](plan/inferred-choices-review.md#table-2). Pairing uses a Noise IKpsk2 handshake keyed by a QR code that expires after 5 minutes or when its screen closes; links race in order; live frames stream only to devices showing the conversation, about 10 a second; when several devices could serve a call, the awake one with the data and grant runs it, then the user's pin (`Bot.homeDeviceId`), then mains power; an unreachable device returns `device_unreachable`, never a denial; stranded commits rejoin like a side thread; every device signs its audit chain head; the relay wakes phones at fire times; notebooks run on the Mac for Mac-only packages and sync their folder to the kernel's device. About 3.5 weeks [INFERENCE].
- [D83](plan/decisions.md#d83): review table 3, the architecture, computers and browsers, amending D49, D73, D74, D76 and D78, and `04-architecture.md` and `06-computers-and-browsers.md`; its 107 outcomes are in the [review record](plan/inferred-choices-review.md#table-3). Routines and watches run in lanes of their own, each under its own lease, and wakes stay in their conversation; each bot has a home Mac (`Bot.homeDeviceId`) that serves Mac-only tools and receives idle leases, and from v1.1 an always-on Mac becomes every bot's home; a recovered step holds its whole conversation. The user can watch and take over a bot's browser from any device; on iPhone a bot keeps at most 2 tabs and 4 in total. Code Mode scripts may call models under a per-script spend cap; up to 8 subagents run at once. Faster targets: cold launch under 2.0 s p95, the daemon ready in 500 ms, a page of history in 50 ms, an approval card in 100 ms on the runner. On the Mac one pinned CPython is shared read-only; iSH ships Alpine's minirootfs, with the Mac's shell, then Blink, then v86 as fallbacks. About 2.5 weeks [INFERENCE]; the review's tally is about 44.25 weeks.
- [D84](plan/decisions.md#d84): review table 4, the platform, bots and the Assistant, amending D23, D25, D31, D76 and D80, and `09-platform-macos.md` and `05-bots-and-assistant.md`; its 95 outcomes are in the [review record](plan/inferred-choices-review.md#table-4). An account-wide monthly cap, set at onboarding, sits over the per-bot budgets; cached tokens don't count toward a run's token cap; a routine's default budget is capped at the bot's monthly limit; side threads spend from a reserved or standby slice; cost previews switch to the user's own medians once there is history. Connector events batch over 60 s (up to 15 minutes under bursts, at most 12 runs an hour); feed watches fire on the first new item; a past-due wake fires once, telling the bot how late; a bot may have 20 routines and 50 watches; a run that forgets to report posts its final answer as a quiet update; background runs wait while the device is hot or in Low Power Mode. Memory fades with kind-specific half-lives, pins are capped by space, conflicts are judged by the on-device model, and consolidation runs on the Chat's runner under its lease epoch. Handles are unique ignoring case, with reserved names such as @assistant; a failing deterministic check blocks a Handoff's acceptance. On the platform: Lockdown Mode sends Python to the Mac, iOS gets the increased-memory-limit and extended-virtual-addressing entitlements, every build uses iCloud's Production environment, and Enhanced Security hardening starts in Phase 1. About 1.5 weeks [INFERENCE].
- [D85](plan/decisions.md#d85): review table 5, the experience, design, coding and security, amending D43, D60, D75 and D77, and `02-experience.md`, `03-design-system.md`, `07-coding.md` and `08-security-and-privacy.md`; its 67 outcomes are in the [review record](plan/inferred-choices-review.md#table-5). Mini apps on the Mac open as a sheet with Open in Window and Full Screen one click away; the iPhone welcome leads with "Pair with your Mac"; phone coding sessions show Changes and Files, with Accept, comments and Commit run on the Mac; a subfolder's AGENTS.md loads when the bot first works there; the Mac bundles signed ripgrep and fd; HTML and Plotly notebook outputs render in the sandboxed artifact view, and a user's cell edit beats a bot's; secret files are invisible to the sandbox; skills and MCP servers get a model review at install that can only add warnings; Kuvo's Frosted/Solid toggle joins the parity set. About 3 weeks [INFERENCE]; the review's tally is about 48.75 weeks.
- [D86](plan/decisions.md#d86): review table 6, the roadmap, risks, Phase 0 and product, amending D55, D59 (the Mac app's bundle ID) and NEXT_VERSION's proposals; its 72 outcomes are in the [review record](plan/inferred-choices-review.md#table-6). The Mac app gets its own bundle ID, `com.mehmetcanbudak.memoshi.mac`, and the iPhone and iPad app keeps `com.mehmetcanbudak.memoshi`; P0-16 creates both, and they are permanent. Track A, a personal Kuvo fork, is dropped. From NEXT_VERSION it adopts QuickAsk in full, with live typed drafts, no keystroke sent to a remote model without disclosure and Undo as a new authorized act; the visual task and artifact views; and the Apple tools, with the WeatherKit framework on the Mac if S5 confirms it and AlarmKit on iPad too. Shared rooms move to v1.1 with Groups, and "routing never grants permission" is a rule from v1. Targets: replica lag p95 under 250 ms direct and 1 s via the relay; relay approvals under 5 s p95; a 10,000-line diff opens in 500 ms; cost previews show a calibrated range; the red-team suite covers every source, mode, tier and origin. Operations: the builder's spend is capped at $300 a month until M2.1; TestFlight uploads nightly whenever main changed; a self-hosted Mac mini runs CI; relay logs hold metadata only for 7 days, and the relay is free and capped for the user and invited testers; a GPL self-check comes before any friend installs a build, and the lawyer before a public release; metrics stay local, and NPS waits for a public release. About 2 weeks [INFERENCE]; the review's tally is about 50.75 weeks.
- [D87](plan/decisions.md#d87): the staged calendar, superseding D70's. v1.0 carries the review's foundations, about 30 of its 51 weeks [INFERENCE], and all earlier v1 scope: Phase 1 runs 19 weeks (W6–W24), Phase 2 35 (W25–W59), Phase 3 40 (W60–W99) and Phase 4, Hardening and v1.0, 6 (W100–W105), so the v1.0 gate is Fri 2028-11-24, after 105 plan weeks. Two gated drops follow as updates on v1.0's channels. **Drop 1, mini app power** (W106–W116, gate Fri 2029-02-23): mini app background jobs and widgets, the signed bytecode cache, the rest of Swift, rich styling, the bot's digest of program actions and the adaptive kit. **Drop 2, devices and polish** (W117–W126, gate Fri 2029-05-04): the open model on the phone, synced browser cookies, browser takeover across devices, coding on iPhone and iPad, notebook extras, live Apple-data cards, the visual views, live QuickAsk drafts and the Apple tools. v1.1 (Phase 5) starts Mon 2029-05-07, sized at the v1.0 gate, and adds shared rooms, Remote machines reachable from any device and published VM image sources. The Swift engine probe moves to W25 and the iSH probe to W60. The user chose this over one gate on about Fri 2029-05-11. **Its calendar is superseded by [D122](plan/decisions.md#d122), and its stage assignments by [D117](plan/decisions.md#d117) where D106–D116 moved features.** The weeks, dates and drop contents in this line are the record of D87; [§4](#4-plan-at-a-glance) has the current ones.

### Reconciliation of 2026-10-23

- [D88](plan/decisions.md#d88): the user chose **Queue until reconnected** rather than moving MOD-09 earlier. With neither a reachable runner nor an available allowed model, messages remain in a restart-safe, cancelable device-local queue, outside canonical history. Original `requestId`s go through normal idempotent forwarding on reconnect; queueing takes no lease and executes no model or tool. DEV-09 stays Phase 3; MOD-09 stays Drop 2. Real model-backed side threads remain supported.
- [D89](plan/decisions.md#d89): adopt Pi_Swift's [Code Mode engine design](../../Pi_Swift/docs/design/code-mode-engine-2026-10-23.md). The same pinned `quickjs.wasm` uses `wasm2c` for shipping and WasmKit for reference/compatibility, with no JavaScriptCore. Node-oracle differential parity and safety gate the default. Memoshi supplies the workload and cold/warm p95 limits by Mon 2026-11-09; both Release backends are measured on the iPhone 14 Pro on Thu 2026-11-12. S1.18 remains required; S1.19 is retired.
- [D90](plan/decisions.md#d90): Phase 0 readiness, left to the recommendation. Pi_Swift's slice-ready state (its requirements §4; `slice-1` planned Fri 10-30) starts P0-05 and P0-06 and Memoshi-Plan's move; before it, adapter code may be written against whatever compiles, but S1 results count only on a `slice-N` tag. The Code Mode record due Mon 11-09 adds a memory ceiling. P0-01 creates a private GitHub repository, whose self-hosted runner sits on the Mac mini or, until then, the development Mac, and never runs jobs for pull requests from forks.
- [D91](plan/decisions.md#d91): the user and all agents may inspect Kuvo's GitHub source and copy/adapt its code for qualifying personal/noncommercial use, preserving terms, notices and revision/path provenance. No separate observer or lawyer-before-reference gate remains. Selective frontend reuse and a personal fork are permitted options, not a selected cutover; Pi_Swift and Memoshi's core contracts stay. Its reopened design/staffing questions are now settled by D92/D93. [D106](plan/decisions.md#d106) (2026-11-13) does not take that reuse for the interface: it is written new, and no Kuvo- or Cognitio-derived source file, asset or string enters this repository.
- [D92](plan/decisions.md#d92): **Viticci-led Apple-native assistant + Kuvo-informed workbench**, one coherent family across devices. This replaces blanket 100/100 Kuvo parity, including shared coding surfaces. P0-19's planned output is `docs/specs/design-reference.md`; scenario-based design review retains accessibility and performance gates. Existing working layouts, Apps, Frosted/Solid and later design-pass stages remain; no new composer/tab arrangement or token numbers are selected. **Superseded as the lead by [D106](plan/decisions.md#d106) (2026-11-13):** Cognitio's design leads the shell on every device, and Viticci's grammar stays for native Apple-data cards, Apps and phone navigation.
- [D93](plan/decisions.md#d93): **no mandatory designer contract**. Builder + coding agents implement with user design review. Targeted external help is optional for a concrete gap and separately chosen scope/cost; design QA remains required, with no automatic date or capacity savings.
- The D88–D90 reconciliation kept per-conversation writers, routine/watch claims, Mac mini-app sheets and D87's staged jobs, widgets, cache and phone model. D91 changes source/reuse policy, D92 design precedence and D93 staffing—not those runtime contracts. S2.12 defines the promised lease-authority load measurement. Approval-card latency and replica lag remain separate metrics. The calendar is unchanged, including the 2028-12-25 → 2029-01-05 break.
- The 548-row review remains historical. Notebook-output commit policy, the checklist's attention behavior and spike-deferred numeric choices remain open where their specs say so; this pass does not invent answers to them.

### Native design preview of 2026-10-25

[D94](plan/decisions.md#d94) selects the user's newer Viticci reference set: Current/Warm palette comparison, floating bot identities, mobile bottom tabs and compact composition, richer Mac navigation with Activity/resource/second-chat context, and refined resource cards. Read [the implementation record](specs/design-reference.md) before UI work. The original **Design Preview · Not connected** remains available separately; the Mac Assistant now also has the [text-only development integration below](#text-chat-development-proof). This does not connect the example resources or certify Apple connectors, real devices, the future Apps section or any roadmap gate.

**Since 2026-11-13 this preview is no longer the lead reference ([D106](plan/decisions.md#d106)).** It is frozen at git tag `design-preview-2026-11-13`. The Phase 1 shell starts from Cognitio's layout, is written new in Memoshi's own code, and reuses Memoshi's own tokens and avatar set where they fit; no Kuvo- or Cognitio-derived interface code enters this repository. [Direction from 2026-11-13](specs/design-reference.md#direction-2026-11-13) in the design reference states the rule now in force; the rest of that record keeps the earlier direction's evidence. Cognitio keeps developing in parallel in its own repository, as the behaviour reference and the oracle for ports ([D107](plan/decisions.md#d107)).

<a id="foundation-implementation-proof"></a>
### Foundation implementation and proof (2026-10-25)

The user authorized the P0-04 → P0-07 → P0-08 foundation. [D95](plan/decisions.md#d95) records the delegated transport choice: public Network.framework has no same-connected-socket credential seam for the required pre-HTTP checks, so **both ends use SwiftNIO exactly 2.104.0**, not a Network/backpressure fallback. These are implementation and observed-proof records, not blanket ticket or Phase 0 passes.

| Implemented source | Current boundary |
|---|---|
| [`MemoshiCore` → `MemoshiStore`](../Packages/MemoshiCore/Sources/Store/) | Actor-owned SQLite WAL, now schema 13 (13 adds the event log's identity, [D131](plan/decisions.md#d131)); transactional metadata/task/card/approval records and global `seq`/conversation `cseq`; committed-event bus, replay/live observation, bounded paging/pruning, pre-migration backups/`quick_check`, newer-schema refusal, daemon `flock` and conversation fences. Schemas 3–8 added chat/policy/approval indexes, plain class-A audit mirroring, outcome receipts, folder grants/copy ownership/export receipts and recovery queues; 9 adds bot/Chat/Session records, 10 child ownership, 11 conversation lifecycle, and 12 gateway audit. Transcripts stay in Pi. Local ownership/grants/queues are not S2 replication; the plain mirror is not a HMAC chain, peer anchoring or legacy-event backfill. |
| [`MemoshiIPC` → `MemoshiTransport`](../Packages/MemoshiIPC/Sources/MemoshiTransport/LocalWebSocket.swift), [Security bridge](../Packages/MemoshiIPC/Sources/CMemoshiSecurity/CMemoshiSecurity.c) | Same-FD uid/audit-token/dynamic-code checks before HTTP handlers or protocol reads/writes, Apple anchor + Team + sealed expected ID + hardened runtime; private paths/socket/token, per-start token rotation, bounded framing/queues. Ad-hoc or relaxed signing fails closed; there is no development bypass. Pinned NIO accepts some non-minimal extended-length encodings; strict rejection is not claimed. |
| [`MemoshiDevice`](../Packages/MemoshiIPC/Sources/MemoshiDevice/) and [`MemoshiDeviceApple`](../Packages/MemoshiIPC/Sources/MemoshiDeviceApple/) | Separate qualification prototype: native MLS, encrypted durable device/authority/mailbox/replica state, confirmed pairing, exact host-enrolled identity roster, membership, relay/direct links, authority-backed leases/committed digests and catch-up. Reachable authority remains required, including on direct paths. This is not provisioned app pairing, durable-cloud/deployed-relay authority, physical-device/Pi-store replication or product device-domain acceptance. [Current scorecard](research/spikes/2026-11-04-s2-devices-draft.md); older [process proof](#multi-conversation-device-proof) retains its original scope. |
| [`MemoshiDaemon`](../Packages/MemoshiDaemon/Sources/MemoshiDaemon/DaemonServer.swift), [`memoshid`](../memoshid/main.swift), [RPC routing](../Packages/MemoshiDaemon/Sources/MemoshiDaemon/RPCSession.swift) | Real helper self-authentication before storage side effects, singleton/listener, hello negotiation, strict requests and bounded replay/focus/backpressure. The development runtime adds bot/Session/chat/model/task inspection, targeted cancellation, local folder grants, fenced user-only `cards.respond` and the exclusive Keychain get broker. Normal CLI composition supplies no tools; no product CLI client role, other host service or device domain exists. Scheduler stays `off`; `ready` is not full engine qualification. |
| [`MemoshiClient`](../Packages/MemoshiClient/Sources/MemoshiClient/), [application receipts](../Packages/MemoshiClient/Sources/MemoshiClient/ClientEvents.swift) | Typed calls, same-FD daemon validation **before token reread on every connect**, single-flight connection/backoff, bounded in-flight/delivery queues, known/opaque-unknown events, dedupe/gap handling. Only `acknowledgeApplied` after actual application advances the cursor. `completeResync` acknowledges the exact bootstrap barrier only after real snapshots have been applied with their `asOf` filters. Hello/status or receipt alone is not bootstrap. No canonical disk cache or automatic mutation retry exists. |
| [Mac supervisor](../apps/mac/Memoshi/DaemonSupervisor.swift), [status UI](../apps/mac/Memoshi/DaemonStatusView.swift), [owned child](../apps/mac/Memoshi/OwnedDaemonProcess.swift) | Validates the actual app/helper bundle and peer, sanitized app-owned foreground lifetime and asynchronous quit veto. Normal Quit/Stop do not SIGKILL; the Development menu's confirmed **Kill memoshid** targets only the supervisor's own authenticated helper PID. Unexpected exits restart at 0.5 s, then 2 s, stopping after three exits in five minutes. The development profile neither registers a LaunchAgent nor offers service controls; actual SMAppService approval/handoff remains unproven. Since 2026-11-21, after Disable Background Service the app waits up to 35 s, just above the agent plist's 30 s `ExitTimeOut`, for the agent's daemon to exit before it starts its own helper; that daemon is identified by the PID and start time of its authenticated hello and is only read, never signalled, and past 35 s the app reports "agent still running" and starts nothing. While the supervisor waits (for a daemon that may already run, for its own helper's start up to 120 s, or for the agent to stop) the popover says what it waits for instead of "The daemon is stopped". Logic tests only (`MemoshiTests/DaemonSupervisorTests`; [work of 2026-11-21](#work-2026-11-21)). iOS instead owns a persistent in-process runtime, exercised separately on an owned Simulator, not through this supervisor. [Current native proof](#multi-conversation-device-proof). |

The [protocol](specs/protocol.md) and [event catalog](specs/events.md#11-contract-fixtures-and-tests) declare future engine/host/device domains; types, fixtures and six additional system payload types are not proof those emitters or services exist. Settings, pruning, migration and stopping are emitted for real implemented transitions. The Swift store re-expresses Draft 3 behavior without importing its TypeScript runtime/source; provenance headers name the original paths at `ce39f8a7ca324ce1c2473518593b1fb4f96ebe4b`. No `LICENSE` was present at that reference revision; the prototype's current working-tree licence is **all rights reserved**, permitting personal build/run, not blanket redistribution; see [`NOTICE`](../NOTICE). P0-17's port audit is complete ([phase-0 §7](plan/phase-0.md#port-list), 2026-11-03): nothing was ported directly, six files carry original-path headers, and the drop list was verified not copied.

#### Observed development proof

These observations were made on this development Mac, not the reference hardware, a production release or a physical iPhone/iPad. Redacted output is retained outside the repository at [`foundation-smoke.txt`](../../evidence/2026-10-25-memoshi-foundation/foundation-smoke.txt); source links above identify the real implementations. The signed replay harness used isolated `/tmp/memoshi-backend.SG7vqT/daemon-data`, **not** the default `~/Library/Application Support/Memoshi`, which already contains active separate Draft 3 prototype data. That unrelated data remains untouched: never delete, chmod or reuse it to make a smoke pass, and do not infer an app connection at that root. Native app checks use a disposable process-only home without changing the production default. Counts below describe separate scopes, not one aggregate acceptance score.

| Exercised scope | Actual result and limit |
|---|---|
| Standalone store reopen/ownership smoke | `schema=2 seq=1 cseq=1 stableDevice=true taskRecovered=true staleFenceRejected=true daemonLockExclusive=true`. This proves the exercised local store paths, not Pi recovery or distributed hand-over. |
| Focused package tests | `swift test --package-path …`: Core 25 tests/6 suites passed; IPC 35/4 passed; Client 16/2 passed; Daemon one real fail-closed unauthorized-self test passed. These counts describe focused scopes; the final integrated CI below also exercises the package suites. |
| Separate qualifying signing | Disposable real-daemon/client harnesses and native app copies used an existing genuine **Apple Development** certificate (Team `476T8NQCSX`), empty entitlements, hardened runtime and actual app/helper signing identifiers; `codesign --verify --strict` passed. Repository defaults/settings/accounts were unchanged. No Developer ID identity was available; membership renewal, profiles and notarization were not inferred. |
| Final integrated CI | After the native service-discovery correction, `scripts/ci.sh` passed all gates in 37.87 s: format/boundaries over 152 Swift files, **121 tests** across six packages, licence scan, canonical schema drift, XcodeGen, Mac + universal iOS-simulator builds, helper layout and root `LICENSE`/`NOTICE` byte-for-byte inside both app/helper bundles. This is build/test/package evidence, not Developer ID/notarization or real-device/recovery proof. |
| Actual production daemon/client replay | Hello negotiated protocol 1 and schema `sha256:bd420f29dab24b49c0921570ff070fbecefb3bfe399509a2b87327eed18cacc6`; an actual empty approval-domain snapshot was applied and acknowledged. After 512 live retention-setting commits and 1,000 disconnect/offline-commit/reconnect/replay cycles: `seq=1512 applied=1512 duplicates=0 gaps=0`, one initial resync, connected, pending 0, no failure. One focused UI frame arrived; ten other-conversation frames were filtered. FDs stayed daemon 14→14/client 5→5. The 7.83 s duration is an observation on this Mac, not a performance-budget pass. |
| Negative authentication/protocol cases | Actual bad token → `unauthorized`; signed app claiming CLI → `forbidden`; unsupported version → `version_mismatch`; ordered ping/status batch; status scheduler `off`/host disconnected; `chats.send` → `method_not_found`. A same-user unapproved Python peer sent an HTTP upgrade and was closed/reset with **0 received bytes**, no HTTP 101 or app data. Actual root/run modes were 0700, socket/token 0600. |
| Graceful stop/restart | Daemon exited 0 and token hash changed without exposing token bytes. The existing client automatically reconnected and replayed stopping plus the next real mutation: `seq=1514 applied=1514 duplicates=0 gaps=0`, one live frame, one total resync, connected, pending 0, no failure. This was **not** a deliberate kill/chaos test. |
| Packaged default ad-hoc helper | Actual `--version`/`--help` reported `memoshid 0.1.0 (build 1)`, protocol 1 and real service usage with models/Apple hosts unconnected. Normal service startup exited **70**, `service_failed: signingRequired`, with no store side effects: the default build fails closed. |
| Separate signed packaged helper | A copied Debug helper initially failed dyld library validation because its generated debug dylib was still ad-hoc. Signing all copied nested Mach-O/debug/preview dylibs inside-out with the same existing Apple Development identity corrected it, with **no disable-library-validation entitlement or source/default-signing change**. Actual startup reported ready/engine unavailable; a second helper exited **75** (`already_running`); owning stdin EOF stopped the first with exit **0**. This does not prove the app's connected foreground lifecycle or SMAppService registration. |
| Actual signed Mac surface and lifetime | Before correction, `SMAppService.notFound` incorrectly blocked a valid bundle; read-only probes returned raw status 3 with plist/helper present under both real and isolated homes, and normal LaunchServices launch reproduced it. The supervisor now validates the bundle independently and permits its foreground helper without registering a background service. The actual signed app showed **Daemon available → Authenticated**, protocol 1, app-owned helper, and **Models and tools not connected / canonical state not loaded**. AX and window captures verified the surface; the status popover stayed open across a status poll. App PID 42756 owned helper PID 42838; normal app quit exited 0 and the helper was gone, without a forced kill. The default ad-hoc app showed **Signing required** and spawned no helper. Background registration controls were not invoked. |

**Scope of the preceding foundation observations:** those runs predate the text integration below. They did not connect preview examples or bootstrap chat state, and their schema-2/121-test figures remain historical. Actual SMAppService registration/approval/handoff, exec/FD-transfer and release-load scenarios, policy/Keychain broker, HMAC audit chain, distributed devices and held recovery/device gates remain unproven; no broader P0-04/P0-07/P0-08, S1, S2, S5 or release pass follows.

<a id="text-chat-development-proof"></a>
### Text-only Mac/Pi development integration (2026-10-25)

The user approved continuing useful adapter work while Pi_Swift finishes. This implements [D90](plan/decisions.md#d90)'s pre-tag development allowance, **not** a Pi slice-ready declaration or completion of P0-05/P0-06/P0-09.

| Implemented boundary | Current behavior |
|---|---|
| [Fixed Pi dependency](../Packages/MemoshiCore/Package.swift), [runtime](../Packages/MemoshiCore/Sources/Runtime/) | SwiftPM checks out committed local candidate `892585fc8d8be3093b58c34aacffcd4e68c3a8d9`, upstream pin `22dc6c26f3c530e11b8cc40443ecdce2b3c84116`, not the sibling's dirty working tree. Only `MemoshiRuntime` imports Pi modules. The sibling Git clone must contain this commit; this workstation-local SCM dependency is not a published/reproducible release pin. Pi_Swift and Memoshi-Plan remain read-only here. Since [D134](plan/decisions.md#d134) (2026-11-14) the pin is Pi_Swift's tag `slice-2`, used by path ([pin move](#d134-pin-move-2026-11-14)). |
| Durable text and metadata | One actual Assistant Chat; PiDurable owns transcript entries. `memoshi.sqlite` stores stable message/task IDs, Pi entry references, turn state and original request receipts, not a second transcript. Repeated sends retain their original IDs; conflicting reuse is refused. History is bounded and cursor-paged. Stop captures its original target, aborts actual Pi work and cannot cancel a later turn on replay. Identity prompts read current Chat metadata per request; full bot-definition/memory services remain future work. |
| Text/provider profile | Explicit loopback OpenAI-compatible configuration, keyless or with a purpose-bound Keychain reference; explicit OpenAI uses only `https://api.openai.com/v1` and requires a reference. PiAI performs actual model discovery and text streaming with real task state/UTF-16 offsets. A missing/failed broker makes that provider unavailable; an unspecified send stays local and never selects a paid fallback. Busy sends are rejected. Tools/model tools are explicitly empty; Code Mode, MCP and Apple connectors remain disabled. The [provisioned native proof](#provisioned-native-keychain-proof) covers the original device-only OS Keychain/Bearer-protected Ollama path; the later [real ChatGPT cloud proof](#real-cloud-chat-proof) covers native account access, cloud inference/Stop and normal reopen. Neither qualifies lock/iCloud behavior. |
| [Mac model](../apps/mac/Memoshi/LiveChatModel.swift), [provider setup](../apps/mac/Memoshi/ModelProviderSetup.swift), [view](../apps/mac/Memoshi/LiveChatView.swift) | Applies actual chat/provider/history/task/approval-domain snapshots before acknowledging the bootstrap barrier, then acknowledges only applied durable receipts. Focus follows visible panes; one consumer serves both mirrored views. Local preferences contain drafts, explicit provider-qualified model choice and uncertain send/provider-configuration intents, including opaque references but never key values or finished transcript text. Since 2026-11-18 they also hold the credential reference a pending provider configuration replaces (`text-chat.pending-provider-superseded`) and the replaced references whose removal has not succeeded yet (`text-chat.replaced-credentials`); on confirmation an API key is removed from the Keychain and a ChatGPT sign-in by sign-out, and a reference that cannot be removed is kept and retried at the next confirmation ([`ModelSetupRequest`](../Packages/MemoshiApp/Sources/AppCore/ModelSetupRequest.swift)). They also hold the saved Send's latest refusal (`text-chat.pending-send-refusal`): both apps keep the saved Send in AppCore's [`SavedSend`](../Packages/MemoshiApp/Sources/AppCore/SavedSend.swift), so a refused Send still offers Discard after a relaunch, and a new request, a retry or a removal clears the reason. Uncertain delivery retries use the original request; this is not D88's full offline queue or side-thread implementation. Unavailable choices remain visible and cannot Send; no paid model is auto-selected. Design examples remain separate behind the toolbar toggle. |
| Storage and lifetime | The app's normal text profile uses `~/Library/Application Support/Memoshi Development/Text Chat`, never the existing prototype's `…/Memoshi` root. `memoshid --development-chat --data-directory …` opts in explicitly and refuses the production root or descendants. It remains an authenticated app-owned helper, with no automatic service registration or signature bypass. |

Actual observations are retained once outside the repo in [`runtime-smoke.txt`](../../evidence/2026-10-25-memoshi-text-chat/runtime-smoke.txt), [`native-smoke.txt`](../../evidence/2026-10-25-memoshi-text-chat/native-smoke.txt) and the adjacent Memoshi window captures:

| Exercised scenario | Observed result |
|---|---|
| Real PiDurable + Ollama `local/qwen3:0.6b` | Arithmetic reply `5`; original send replay returned the same identities without changing history; Stop after a real text delta produced task `canceled`; graceful close/reopen preserved exact four-message history and Chat identity. The first run exposed a reserved `pi.user` input kind; changing the adapter to application-owned `memoshi.user` made the live run pass without changing Pi. |
| Four new store regressions | Reopened request identity/conflict refusal; duplicate/stale projection and page boundaries; busy admission and old Stop target; old provider-config replay not reverting a newer setting. |
| Integrated repository checks | `scripts/ci.sh` passed: 166 Swift files, **125 tests across six packages**, 27 resolved dependency occurrences accepted by the licence scan, schema drift, Mac/universal iOS Simulator builds and bundle/notices checks. Final source checks and native evidence are recorded in the adjacent proof files; test counts are not an S1 gate. |
| Actual signed Mac surface | Genuine Apple Development-signed copied app/helper, Team `476T8NQCSX`, hardened runtime and unchanged repository signing defaults. Real arithmetic reply `5`; explicit Send streamed a long answer, then native Stop settled its partial text with **Stopped** and durable task `canceled`. Current/Warm switching preserved history; Design Preview hid real messages and restored them unchanged; the second Assistant pane mirrored the same IDs/text rather than creating another Chat. |
| Actual normal quit/reopen | App PID 25364 owned helper 25379; a process-exit event confirmed normal app termination and the old helper was gone. App PID 34566 reopened the same isolated root with **all five existing message IDs and texts unchanged**, including earlier test history. This is not the separate four-message runtime smoke and is not a deliberate kill test. |

The native proof uses the disposable process-only home `…/T/memoshi-native-proof-u4hulf4y`; its actual helper arguments and full data path are in `native-smoke.txt`. Conversation stores are preserved. No prototype data was opened, migrated, deleted or chmodded. Native automation had text-entry/settling limitations; observed Send/Stop, applied state and captures—not an immediate AX setter return—supply the proof.

**Still gated:** a qualifying `slice-N`; complete runtime/conformance and all-call audit coverage, standing rules/grant management, high-risk drafts and verified device-owner authorization, remaining Keychain lock/iCloud qualification, Code Mode shipping/parity, MCP, production tools, Apple connectors, budgets, distributed leases, iOS engine, keyboard/VoiceOver/performance review, SMAppService registration, Developer ID/notarization and every held kill/real-device scenario. The bounded policy/approval implementation and later [real-cloud smoke](#real-cloud-chat-proof) are not those gates. D89's `wasm2c` shipping choice is unchanged; linked WasmKit code is not backend qualification. P0-05/P0-06/P0-20 and Phase 0 remain open.

#### Runtime contract follow-through (2026-10-26)

- **Receipt fix:** the existing [store regression](../Packages/MemoshiCore/Tests/MemoshiStoreTests/ChatStoreTests.swift) reproduced `writtenSeq` changing from 1 to 6 after completion. Later projections no longer advance that sequence or rewrite the cached send result; replay retains the original receipt. Current task/history snapshots still carry their own watermarks. [Failing-before/passing-after evidence](../../evidence/2026-10-25-memoshi-runtime-contracts/receipt-regression.txt).
- **Four [runtime contracts](../Packages/MemoshiCore/Tests/MemoshiRuntimeTests/RuntimeContractTests.swift):** real PiDurable and SQLite with only a scripted loopback HTTP provider. Covers original receipt/exact history after reopen with provider offline; sanitized HTTP failure without retry and a usable next turn; interrupted SSE retaining failed partial text across reopen; old Stop replay not canceling a newer turn, including emoji/combining UTF-16 offsets. No Pi SDK module is imported by the tests. SwiftNIO remains exact 2.104.0 and is test-only here; `NOTICE` records that use.
- **Two [client streaming contracts](../Packages/MemoshiClient/Tests/MemoshiClientTests/ClientStreamingTests.swift):** UI-only text waits for consumer-applied durable facts without blocking them; reconnect drops held old-connection partials and replays from the applied cursor. Synthetic carriers are not authentication/signing proof.
- **Verification:** [integrated checks](../../evidence/2026-10-25-memoshi-runtime-contracts/final-checks.txt) passed: 170 Swift files, **131 tests across six packages**, 31 accepted dependency occurrences, schema checks, Mac/universal iOS Simulator builds and bundle/notices checks. The [actual Ollama smoke](../../evidence/2026-10-25-memoshi-runtime-contracts/runtime-smoke.txt) returned `5`, preserved original receipt `writtenSeq: 2` after settlement, canceled after a real text delta, and preserved exact four-message history and the original receipt through graceful reopen. Its initial unbounded observer failure and correction are retained in the evidence; the throwaway harness was removed. No new native UI, device or deliberate-kill proof is claimed.
- **Follow-through:** the provisioned Keychain proof below resolved the Mac development profile/device-only-path blocker; the [policy/approval development proof](#policy-approval-development-proof) now exercises the next adapter slice. The immutable Pi pin/release block, empty production tool set and held device/recovery gates remain unchanged.

<a id="keychain-provider-broker-proof"></a>
#### Keychain/provider broker follow-through (2026-10-26)

- **Implemented:** [app-owned Keychain actor](../Packages/MemoshiApp/Sources/AppCore/ModelKeychain.swift), [native settings/key sheet](../apps/mac/Memoshi/ModelSetupView.swift), [provider intents](../apps/mac/Memoshi/ModelProviderSetup.swift), [bounded client reverse calls](../Packages/MemoshiClient/Sources/MemoshiClient/ClientHostCalls.swift), [daemon reverse routing](../Packages/MemoshiDaemon/Sources/MemoshiDaemon/RPCSession+Keychain.swift) and [per-use Pi credentials/HTTP lease checks](../Packages/MemoshiCore/Sources/Runtime/ProviderCredentials.swift). The app alone has an optional secret-group entitlement; the helper never receives it. Keychain items bind an opaque reference to one model provider/purpose. No fallback to legacy/default Keychain, environment credentials, implicit keyless use or paid model selection is added.
- **Consumer contracts:** nine [Keychain boundary tests](../Packages/MemoshiApp/Tests/MemoshiAppTests/ModelKeychainTests.swift), sixteen [client host tests](../Packages/MemoshiClient/Tests/MemoshiClientTests/ClientKeychainHostTests.swift), eight [real Pi/HTTP credential tests](../Packages/MemoshiCore/Tests/MemoshiRuntimeTests/RuntimeCredentialContractTests.swift) and a store reference/reopen regression. Reproduced and fixed streamed response/stop/reasoning-signature metadata leaking into durable Pi state, Stop not canceling a held credential lookup, and an expired release being reused for HTTP discovery fallback. Safe transcript text remains intact; provider error bodies and opaque metadata are not retained.
- **Integrated verification:** final `scripts/ci.sh` passed after temporary native diagnostics were removed: 182 Swift files, **165 tests across six packages**, 31 accepted dependency occurrences, canonical schema checks, XcodeGen, Mac/universal iOS Simulator builds and bundle/notices checks. [Check record](../../evidence/2026-10-25-memoshi-keychain-broker/final-checks.txt).
- **Actual signed broker/provider smoke:** the [retained carrier output](../../evidence/2026-10-25-memoshi-keychain-broker/signed-carrier-smoke.txt) uses real `MemoshiClient`, transport, same-FD peer checks, packaged helper, Pi and authenticated loopback HTTP; only the external Keychain boundary is memory-only. It proved one exclusive host, another app UI-only, ordinary ping while a release is held, host loss canceling discovery, replacement host, fresh Bearer rotation, real text/completion and original receipt replay, and sanitized echoed HTTP errors. Three paired reverse results caused six fresh carrier checks and zero late sends/UI-only handler calls. Owning stdin EOF exited the helper 0 without signals; raw/base64 bytes of all three disposable canaries were absent from eight fresh daemon-root files. This is not an OS Keychain success, a real cloud call or a shipping-native-app broker round trip.
- **Pre-profile native boundary:** the [native record and captures](../../evidence/2026-10-25-memoshi-keychain-broker/native-proof.txt) show authenticated Model Settings and Add Model Key, masked local/device-only canary input, and the real OS refusal: “Model Keychain access requires the app's authorized access-group entitlement and provisioning profile.” A fresh LaunchServices copy verified the provider-neutral text-only labels, cancellation of its key sheet and normal app/helper teardown; the original copy exited 0. Raw/base64 canary bytes were absent from 17 files across the two fresh proof homes. AX value/keyboard injection limitations are recorded, not counted as a second successful save. No user's key, prototype directory, account setting or installed profile was changed during that pre-profile run.
- **Profile-gate history:** the pre-provisioning read-only inspection found **437 installed profiles and none authorizing `476T8NQCSX.com.mehmetcanbudak.memoshi.mac`**. Apollo/other app profiles were not substituted. Subsequent authorized Xcode provisioning and the real native proof below resolve the Mac development profile/device-only round trip, without a legacy Keychain fallback. Remaining lock/iCloud and security/release qualification keep **P0-20, S1, S5 and Phase 0 open**. Default ad-hoc signing remains fail-closed; Pi_Swift is unchanged/read-only, and tools, policy, Apple connectors and iOS runtime remain off.

<a id="keychain-provisioning-setup"></a>
#### Profile provisioning continuation (2026-10-26)

- **Authorized scope:** obtain Memoshi's own Mac development profile on the existing team `476T8NQCSX`, then exercise the real app-owned Keychain path. No membership purchase, Apple-account/password/security/billing reconfiguration, certificate revocation, borrowed Apollo identifier/profile, physical-device/kill gate or authentication bypass is authorized by this step.
- **Observed build settings:** a session-only `CODE_SIGN_STYLE` selection evaluates to `Automatic` for `Memoshi` and `Manual` for `memoshid`. The app references `Memoshi/MemoshiKeychain.entitlements`; the helper evaluates with no entitlement file and no profile specifier. Both retain hardened runtime, and base Debug entitlement injection is disabled. Repository/XcodeGen signing defaults remain manual/ad-hoc.
- **Authentication resolved:** the initial automatic-signing fingerprint conflict and Apple's rejection of the cached Xcode login are retained in the [sanitized attempt record](../../evidence/2026-10-26-memoshi-provisioning/provisioning-attempt.txt). Automatic signing uses the `Apple Development` identity class. After the user completed private Xcode reauthentication, provisioning advanced; no password, two-factor code, browser cookie or Xcode credential was extracted.
- **Resource recovery:** the first post-authentication compile hit `ENOSPC`. Only this project's regenerable Xcode intermediates/module caches and the six packages' `.build/out` outputs were reclaimed; sources, dependency checkouts/prebuilts, installed models, existing app copies, profiles and conversations were preserved. The subsequent arm64-only, two-job Release build succeeded in **195.11 seconds**.
- **Executed app-only command:** the command below ran with an explicit allowlisted credential-free build environment. `-allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration` was not passed. It obtained an Apple-managed Mac development profile; the actual embedded profile and sealed app/helper entitlements were inspected before any canary Save. Repository signing defaults are unchanged. The observed profile and native/runtime proof follow; lock/iCloud and S5 release/device qualification are not inferred.

```sh
xcodebuild build -quiet -hideShellScriptEnvironment -jobs 2 \
  -project apps/mac/Memoshi.xcodeproj -scheme Memoshi \
  -configuration Release -destination generic/platform=macOS \
  -derivedDataPath build/DerivedData -disableAutomaticPackageResolution \
  -allowProvisioningUpdates \
  'CODE_SIGN_STYLE=$(MEMOSHI_PROVISION_STYLE_$(TARGET_NAME))' \
  MEMOSHI_PROVISION_STYLE_Memoshi=Automatic \
  MEMOSHI_PROVISION_STYLE_memoshid=Manual \
  'CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY=Apple Development' DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=476T8NQCSX \
  MEMOSHI_KEYCHAIN_ENTITLEMENTS_FILE=Memoshi/MemoshiKeychain.entitlements \
```
