The 2026-11-07 post-format focused runs pass [four Core tests](../../evidence/2026-11-06-memoshi-pstack-safety.3KovFdGh/final_core_after_style.log),
[18 Device tests](../../evidence/2026-11-06-memoshi-pstack-safety.3KovFdGh/final_device_after_style.log),
[20 qualification tests](../../evidence/2026-11-06-memoshi-pstack-safety.3KovFdGh/qualification_after_failed_relay_retention.log)
and [17 Client tests](../../evidence/2026-11-06-memoshi-pstack-safety.3KovFdGh/final_client_after_style.log).
The existing SwiftPM engine compiles current source. Core, Device and qualification checks
disable debug symbols and indexing. These focused checks are not full canonical CI.

The [gate record](plan/gates/phase-0.md) remains No-go. Local source repairs do not supply
SDK adoption, physical devices, deployed services, approved accounts/sites, reference
measurements, one-week dogfood or the prescribed demos.

### Engineering follow-through (2026-11-07)

Local commit `e75a96c` (not pushed then; pushed since) records all earlier uncommitted work. Unchanged canonical `scripts/ci.sh` passed every step at that exact commit over two runs in a clean worktree with its own build root.
- [The first run](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/canonical-e75a96c-01/ci.txt) passed 23 release-tooling tests, 676 package tests, licences, schema drift and project generation, then hit the runner's four-hour limit during the app build.
- [The second run](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/canonical-e75a96c-02-final-steps/run.json) finished the app builds, sign-in flows and bundle layout.

The Pyodide runtime is no longer committed. Its `manifest.json` is the lock, and `scripts/ci.sh` recreates the runtime with the hash-checked `scripts/package-pyodide.py`.

Five read-only analyses informed the work below. They are archived in [`analysis/`](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/analysis/). The user chose the browser boundary, the lease clock, internal-disk S2 storage and strict secret rules, recorded as [D101](plan/decisions.md#d101)–[D103](plan/decisions.md#d103).

| Work | Evidence and limit |
|---|---|
| S2 lease under storage stalls ([D102](plan/decisions.md#d102)) | The root cause was storage stalls on the USB disk-image build volume. Renewals that arrived in time were judged after expiry, then fenced; fencing held with no duplicate grants ([diagnosis](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/analysis/s2-renewal-diagnosis.md)). The relay now judges claim and renewal expiry at frame arrival. The evidence journal writes in the background, and `s2-measurements.json` gains a `storage` block. Each change has a failing-before and passing-after test: 29 MemoshiIPC and 21 MemoshiDaemon tests ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/S2LeaseFix/)). Three `--quick` cohorts on the internal disk pass with unchanged limits (exit 0, fsync p50 about 0.05–0.08 ms, none over 1 s). Earlier failed cohorts stay failures. Bounded-latency storage is part of the result: arrival time cannot save a lease if the authority's own write stalls past the TTL. |
| S4 export and secret-profile controls ([D103](plan/decisions.md#d103)) | Before the fix, approved deletes and replaces destroyed outside edits made after the final conflict check. 20 of 31 secret spellings and every secret-directory case were readable in the sandbox. After: export moves or swaps and verifies before removing, restoring and refusing on a mismatch. Private cleanup quarantines each entry before removal. The profile's secret rules are generated from the host spelling list and cover secret-directory descendants. The real-profile proof records 0 failures in 108 secret rows and 14 offline git/npm/pip/cargo/swift workflow rows ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/S4Controls/)). The [final focused suites](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/parent-queue/07-s4-suites.log) pass 40 tests in 5 suites. That covers create, parent swap, admission swap, cleanup swap, native services, listeners and proxy-ignoring clients. H3, kernel attribution, TCC and the reference rows stay open. |
| Bot browser boundary ([D101](plan/decisions.md#d101)) | Bot web views on Mac and iOS add a deny-local rule list and Lockdown Mode to the gateway route. Loads fail closed until all three are installed. 9 focused tests pass, including real WebKit blocking of loopback, IPv6, `localhost`, numeric and userinfo targets, an exact fixture exemption, Lockdown removing WebRTC and WebTransport, and granted pages reading their own files ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/BrowserBoundary/)). The real product browser files ran through the [independent-sink control](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/BrowserBoundary/control-boundary3-f5ecba3816d3/) (`spikes/S3-webkit-browser/no-bypass/`). Before, at `e75a96c`, they leaked 259 fixture-route, 233 proxy-route and 20 service-worker sink events and failed the fail-closed checks. After, the product pages load and run their probes (95 and 124 results) with 0 sink events, every fail-closed check passes, and the positive controls still reach the sinks. The `target=_blank` empty loopback connection (3 events) is a retained failing control, so the strict verdict stays false. The owned upload/download suite then exposed a real defect: block-all also blocked `blob:` URLs, so granted pages could not read uploaded files. Allowlists now exempt each granted origin's own `blob:` URLs; [the test](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/BrowserRepro/10-boundary-tests-before-blob-fix.log) failed before and passes after. The [suite passes 7/7 under the boundary](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/BrowserRepro/) with no setup failures. Its stalls traced to WebKit's persistent store on the nearly full external build drive; they reproduced before the boundary, and the repro's home now lives on the internal disk. No real sites were visited. |
| Native accessibility | The stream state is an accessibility value, and approval cards are one summarised heading element with actions. The design system adds an announcer, `accent.text` tint and `border.strong` under Increase Contrast. Mac keyboard paths cover Waiting for You, ⌘J, F6 and Move to Trash. 20 DesignSystem tests pass, as do 146 contrast pairs in both palettes and all appearances. The real Mac and iOS apps build. The rendered AX and key-event repro fails at `e75a96c` ([before](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/A11yFixes/native/native-repro-a11ybeforer6-874f5397/chat/result.json)): chat semantics in all four palette and appearance combinations, keyboard and Trash. It passes every case [after](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/A11yFixes/native/native-repro-a11yafterr6-e1d3866f/chat/result.json). A reported card-in-transcript flattening was later shown to be a harness artifact; see the 2026-11-08 round. Spoken VoiceOver, Full Keyboard Access, Voice Control and iPad checks are operator-only. |
| SDK adoption | The [adoption plan](research/spikes/2026-11-07-pi-swift-adoption-plan.md) maps H0–H4 and the Apple host to both codebases in units U1–U9. A dated request to the SDK owner, `docs/requests/2026-11-07-memoshi-slice-adoption.md` in the verification workspace, asks for the tag, published CI evidence, migration notes and frozen APIs, and lists the open gaps. The pin stays `892585fc`. Since [D134](plan/decisions.md#d134) (2026-11-14) it is the tag `slice-2`. |

Unchanged canonical `scripts/ci.sh` then [passed on the final tree](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/canonical-final-tree-02/run.json): 715 Swift tests in 15 runs, 23 release-tooling tests and every gate through bundle layout, in 3918.94 s. That tree is committed after `e75a96c`. The final [no-bypass control rerun](../../evidence/2026-11-07-memoshi-engineering/BrowserBoundary/control-final-f51fbf0c3990/) on that code repeats the result: product scenarios at 0 sink events, from 258, 241 and 20 before. The gate stays No-go.

### Engineering follow-ups (2026-11-08)

`e99946e` is pushed. Builds now use an owned internal-disk build root. The harnesses read `MEMOSHI_BUILD_ROOT` instead of the external USB volume, which stalled and filled up.

| Work | Evidence and limit |
|---|---|
| S2 lease timing ([D104](plan/decisions.md#d104)) | Renewals extend by the claimed TTL, not the node default. Local store lease writes are ordered per conversation and no longer hold up the renewal loop. Before the fix, 53 of 108 renewals in a retained cohort asked for a different TTL, and a held store write stopped all renewals. After it, 88 Device, 28 DeviceApple and 11 qualification tests pass. Three internal quick cohorts exit 0 with 0 of 146–148 relay renewals off their claimed TTL and no duplicate grants ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/S2Followups/)). The CloudKit authority cannot get D102's arrival-time rule; cross-device clock skew stays an O10 risk. |
| S4 export without swap ([D103 amendment](plan/decisions.md#d103)) | HFS+ replace and delete use an exclusive move-aside, verify and exclusive install. exFAT and FAT32 refuse every export before any effect; FAT32 silently treats both rename flags as an ordinary overwrite. Capability comes from the volume's report, not errno. Failing-before logs, then 31 tests and 132 cases pass on six real volumes: two APFS, HFS+, two exFAT and FAT32 ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/S4SwapFallback/)). `/Volumes/MEW0` is an APFS image on the exFAT drive. |
| Native accessibility, round 2 | The reported card-in-transcript flattening is a harness artifact. With the authorized AX client attached, messages and cards keep their full semantics at `e99946e` and after this round. Native AX evidence counts only with that client attached, and the Mac's largest-text check uses the Larger Text display canvas, because macOS SwiftUI applies no Dynamic Type to text styles. Further fixes: sheets fit 1024 × 600 pt, small Mac controls are ≥ 24 pt, and security text wraps. The default button failed at 2.79:1 in Current dark; `designDefaultAction()` now measures 4.58–8.57:1. English String Catalogs extract 1050 Mac and 274 iOS keys with plurals, and code and IDs are forced left-to-right. 22 DesignSystem tests and the token check pass, both apps build, and the Mac AX repro fails before and passes after ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/A11yRound2/)). iOS large-text layout is source- and build-checked only; spoken VoiceOver is operator-only. |
| Browser follow-ups ([D101 amendment](plan/decisions.md#d101)) | No leak was found, so there was no product change. Under all three list sets: `data:` documents and their loads, the Mac's own private and ULA addresses, and alternate loopback spellings (`127.1`, hex, octal, decimal, IPv4-mapped, trailing dot) all made 0 connections. Without lists, every one reached its sink. With the proxy alone, WebKit sends every IP literal directly; deny-local blocks them. 172.16/12, 169.254/16, `fe80` and `.local` are verified at rule level only. 51 focused tests pass. The extended no-bypass control passes on HEAD and the worktree (product scenarios at 0 sink events, 6/6 fail-closed checks), with `target=_blank` retained. An owned iOS Simulator carrier runs the real `MobileBrowserReviewSession` and passes 54/54. Upload/download passes 7/7 ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/BrowserFollowups/)). The Simulator cannot prove device networking, system Lockdown, a signed build or real sites. |
| Python per-bot realm ([D105](plan/decisions.md#d105)) | Nested workers are refused: `worker.mjs` deletes `Worker` and `SharedWorker` before any interpreter code runs. Native network admission belongs to the cell that started the work: each cell runs as an asyncio task carrying that cell's token, and requests from other contexts are refused before reaching native. On an exact pre-change copy in real WebKit, a nested worker ran 24 beats after its cell returned, and both leftover requests were accepted under the later cell's ID. After: none. 20 `PyodideSpikeTests` pass, and the real-host carriers confirm before and after ([S6 §2f](research/spikes/2026-11-04-s6-pyodide-draft.md#2f-nested-workers-and-cross-cell-borrowing-2026-11-08-development-measurement), [evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/PythonLifecycle/)). Startup samples ran under heavy unrelated load and are not timing evidence. The S6 physical-device rows remain open. |

Unchanged canonical `scripts/ci.sh` [passed on this tree](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/canonical-final-tree-01/run.json): 728 Swift tests in 15 runs, 23 release-tooling tests and every gate through bundle layout, in 1211.84 s on the internal-disk build root. The gate stays No-go.

### Remaining engineering items (2026-11-08, second round)

| Work | Evidence and limit |
|---|---|
| Device journal off the node actor ([D104 amendment](plan/decisions.md#d104)) | Journal appends run on one ordered writer. Renewal records are not awaited, and commits wait without blocking the node. Lease decisions and replica applies still block until durable. Before the fix, a held journal write left the node unresponsive with 0 renewals in 400 ms. After it, 90 Device, 28 DeviceApple and 54 qualification tests pass, and three internal-disk quick cohorts exit 0 ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/S2Round3/)). |
| Stalled-disk S2 control | Three quick cohorts on the USB disk image exit 1. The volume stalled 33–96 s, past the 6 s TTL, and the authority evaluated the burst's renewals only when each stall ended. The Mac lost its leases, with no duplicate grants. This is D102's stated limit, not a new defect; S2 qualification still needs bounded-latency storage. |
| iOS large text and Mac sidebar | An owned iOS carrier rendered the real chat views at accessibility5 and Large on iPhone 18 Pro Max and iPhone Duo. Before: 39–50 failures per AX5 case, plus default-size overlap of the Development Review menu. After: 0 failures in every case on both devices ([run-after5](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/A11yFinish/ios/)). Fixes: the menu moved to its own row, status lines scroll and wrap at accessibility sizes, the card header stacks, and the identity name no longer hyphenates. Mac sidebar actions are icon-only regular controls (≥ 24 pt) with their titles as AX label and help. They fit at 360, 280 and 240 pt in both palettes and appearances, where HEAD truncated them to "New S…". Limit: the Mac window minimum (1040 × 700) is larger than the Larger Text canvas (1024 × 665); that is a design decision for the user. |
| DesignSystem String Catalog | DesignSystem strings resolve from the package bundle's own catalog (217 keys), with plural rules for "%lld items need you" and the card expiry, in both apps. The new test failed on HEAD; after the change 25 DesignSystem tests pass, the token check passes, and each app bundle carries the compiled stringsdict. |
| Browser addresses and gateway authentication ([D81](plan/decisions.md#d81) and [D101](plan/decisions.md#d101) amendments) | Every earlier control's gateway decisions were `unattributed`, because WebKit sends its proxy credential only after a 407 and the gateway answered 403, so no granted site had loaded. The gateway now challenges missing credentials once per connection; stale credentials stay refused. Real WebKit now authenticates and loads a granted owned-fixture host. Loopback, private and rebound answers and `.local` names are refused with 0 independent-sink connections. `.local` and zoneless `fe80` are connect-tested; 169.254/16 and 172.16/12 are not connect-testable without root, and their real main-frame loads are content-blocked. 51 egress, 54 browser and 13 sandbox tests pass. The no-bypass control keeps product scenarios at 0 sink events, with `target=_blank` retained, and upload/download passes 7/7 ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/GatewayAuth/), [round 3](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/BrowserRound3/)). During earlier runs a few empty preconnects to non-owned private addresses, and one 4 s LAN mDNS announcement of an owned name, reached the local network; the tests no longer issue them. |
| Python realm admission and reruns ([D105 amendment](plan/decisions.md#d105)) | Admission is bound to the cell's own running asyncio tasks, recorded outside Python. On an exact copy of the earlier worker, leftover work borrowed a later cell's admission six ways; the new test fails there on its assertions (7 issues). On the working tree all 22 `PyodideSpikeTests` pass. Ordinary networking did not regress: 22 admissions across `pyfetch`, `js.fetch`, cell tasks, `gather`, `TaskGroup`, `wait_for` and post-`sleep` requests, each under its own cell, on both workers. The §2c sealed-network scenario passes (48.34 s) with requests started from Python, and the 20-call scientific driver passes (29.0 s) at low load. The earlier `unsafePath` came from the harness's bot root under `~/Documents`, which macOS refused to open; the harness now uses `/private/tmp` ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/PythonFinish/), [round 2](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/PythonRound2/)). Interpreter-memory writes remain bounded only by native limits, and S6 physical-device rows remain open. |

Unchanged canonical `scripts/ci.sh` [passed on this tree](../../evidence/2026-11-08-memoshi-engineering/canonical-final-tree-02/run.json): 746 Swift tests in 15 runs, 23 release-tooling tests and every gate through bundle layout, in 968.79 s. The gate stays No-go.

<a id="code-review-2026-11-13"></a>
### Code review and fixes (2026-11-13)

**Everything in this section was committed locally on 2026-11-13, in logical commits on `main` after `c3c6c3d`: `c119597` (protocol), `8512182` (core), `52b1253` (IPC), `26cf615` (daemon and client), `b26140f` (app package), `b89d4df` (apps and spikes), `6ccf53c` (tooling), then the token source and the documents. They were pushed to `origin/main` later the same day.** The [review record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-code-review/README.md), retained outside the repository, holds the findings, the ledgers, the verification, the CI logs and the full list of open decisions. It is a development record, not a canonical evidence run. The canonical records above describe commit `c3c6c3d` and are unchanged. The gate stays No-go.

- **Review:** a read-only code review of every package, both apps, the scripts and the workflows produced 166 findings: 1 blocker, 24 high, 82 medium and 59 low. Each finding was then handled test-first by a fixer, and independent verifiers re-read the fixes in up to three passes.
- **Result, by ledger row** (some findings split into several rows, and some rows were added during the work): at the end of the review, 191 fixed with a regression test; 20 fixed but not testable here; 3 not defects; 13 waiting for the owner's decision; 5 deferred with a reason. The 13 were decided the same day (below). Of the untestable fixes, most are in the Mac and iOS app sources, which have no test target and were verified by build; a few need a device, CloudKit or a signed build.
- **The blocker** was in the CloudKit feed path. CryptoKit Ed25519 signatures differ on every call, so a re-signed seal was refused as a conflicting replay and publication wedged. The device relay authority had the same root cause. Both now compare signed content.
- **Checks:** before the fixes, 746 Swift tests and 23 release-tooling tests passed. After them, [`scripts/ci.sh`](../scripts/ci.sh) passed every step with 962 Swift tests and 50 release-tooling tests. After the changes for D123–D130, it passes every step with **981 Swift tests and 66 release-tooling tests**, run on the internal disk without `MEMOSHI_BUILD_ROOT` on the tree that was then committed ([logs](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-code-review/ci/)). Two tooling files changed after that run's tooling step; the tooling tests, the licence scan and the lint were rerun on the final tree and pass. An earlier run the same hour failed only the WebKit Python tests, by timeout, while another session's CPU load script was running. **This is a development check, not a canonical evidence run.**

**Behaviour changes a contributor must know:**

- `RPCErrorData.retryable` is derived from the error kind, as [protocol.md §10](specs/protocol.md#10-errors) states. The old initialiser is gone.
- `session.purged.by` is an enum.
- A `SystemCancelNotification` type and fixture exist.
- The daemon closes clients with 4503 on a graceful stop.
- The local socket is bound as `run/memoshid.bind` and renamed to `memoshid.sock`.
- The device link handshake has one more frame, the initiator's key confirmation, in [`DeviceLink`](../Packages/MemoshiIPC/Sources/MemoshiDevice/DeviceLink.swift) and [`AppleDirectCarrier`](../Packages/MemoshiIPC/Sources/MemoshiDeviceApple/AppleDirectCarrier.swift).
- [`EgressGateway.decisions`](../Packages/MemoshiCore/Sources/Egress/EgressGateway.swift) is a window of the newest 4,096 decisions, and `decisionCount` is cumulative. The gateway refuses a bound credential over cleartext http.
- `trash.empty` purges the whole Trash in batches under one request, and `trash.emptied.by` is `user` or `system`.
- When the data root is too long for a socket path, the daemon socket is `memoshid-<digest>.sock` in the user's private temporary folder, never in `/tmp` ([D130](plan/decisions.md#d130)).
- A host call the daemon cancels keeps its slot until the app answers `canceled` ([protocol.md §6](specs/protocol.md#6-host-capability-methods-daemon-to-app)).
- The schema states each error kind's `retryable` value; decoders accept a contradicting flag and report the kind's value.
- A first device enrollment marks its identity pending until `completeEnrollment()`; only a pending identity can be removed.
- Design tokens are `4.0.0-draft.11`: corrected avatar name colours and the identity badge ([D127](plan/decisions.md#d127)).
- The licence scan checks Rust crates against one allowlist and the Python packages against [`scripts/pyodide-licenses.json`](../scripts/pyodide-licenses.json) ([D126](plan/decisions.md#d126)). A release is refused until the retained Python licence texts in [`Packages/MemoshiApp/LicenseSources/`](../Packages/MemoshiApp/LicenseSources/) are bundled with the runtime.
- `scripts/ci.sh` gained the step "Checked-in native MLS outputs" ([`scripts/check-mls-generated.sh`](../scripts/check-mls-generated.sh)).
- CI no longer runs on `pull_request` ([D90](plan/decisions.md#d90)).

**Evidence impact on S2.** The qualification fixes exposed two weak points in the recorded S2 runs. The claim-level and link-level revocation refusals were the revoked device's own guards; only the relay refusal was real. The 2026-11-03 runs report `audit.expiry.commits: 0`, so their expiry two-writer audit was vacuous. The harness is now stricter. The recorded evidence is unchanged. The rerun this called for was made the same day and passed ([below](#d131-work-2026-11-13)); the filed [gate record](plan/gates/phase-0.md) is not edited for it, and the gate review reads the new record. (Two of that record's D99 rows were edited in place the same day, in `ddab24f`; a note in the record says so since 2026-11-18.)

**The open points were decided the same day** as [D123–D130](plan/decisions.md#d123): the S2 rerun (D123), the two device holds (D124), the Mac camera entitlement (D125), the licence rule (D126), the two contrast fixes (D127), the memory flush before compaction (D128), the permission texts (D129) and the smaller engineering points (D130). The code changes those decisions need were applied, checked by independent reviewers in two passes, and are in the same commits. The two device holds (D124) and the Phase 1 items D130 lists were not changed. The [review record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-code-review/README.md#open-decisions-and-deferred-items) keeps the options that were on the table and the items deferred to Phase 1.

<a id="d131-work-2026-11-13"></a>
### Remaining open items done (2026-11-13, D131)

**By the user's instruction ([D131](plan/decisions.md#d131)) the items the code review had left open were built the same day.** They are fixes to existing behaviour; no product feature started. The code is in commits `1b29505` (device layer), `19b2507` (protocol, store, daemon, client, qualification harness) and `19ed38b` (app package, apps, CI), local on `main`. The [review record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-code-review/README.md) holds the ledgers and the verification for this round too.

- **Host-call deadline.** When the app's own 10-second deadline passes, it answers `timeout` only once the handler has ended; the slot stays taken until then ([protocol.md §6](specs/protocol.md#6-host-capability-methods-daemon-to-app)).
- **Restored history.** The store is at schema 13 and keeps an identity of its event log. `system.hello` carries it as `logId`; `events.subscribe` takes `afterTs` and returns `headTs`. A client that meets another log, or whose stated time does not match the daemon's event at that position, or cannot be checked, gets a resync and never replays another history ([protocol.md §8](specs/protocol.md#8-replay-and-snapshots)).
- **Device holds** ([D124](plan/decisions.md#d124)). A message sealed for a closed group state is retired with its plaintext held and sealed again in the next state under the same operation ID, on the direct, relay and CloudKit routes. A writer may cancel a reservation when its own store refused at the fence or when it never saw the grant, and a removed writer's reservation ends with its leases ([protocol.md §12.15](specs/protocol.md#12-device-layer)).
- **Relay-route handshake.** The 10-second limit for the last handshake frame counts from the first frame on both routes.
- **CloudKit transport.** Old de-duplication history is retired below saved marks, so the lifetime cap of 16,384 messages is gone and a retired entry cannot let a replay in.
- **A send race in the device link**, older than this work and found by the review: a send the carrier refused used up the link's Noise counter, and a TCP send after close was accepted and dropped. Either made a device drop its relay link; no message was lost. Both are fixed.
- **App package.** AppCore, ConversationFeature and ShellFeature have their own String Catalogs. A refused Send's explanation has its own property and stays visible next to a later problem.
- **Browser lab.** "Reset Cookie Probes…" starts a new numbered generation after reading and recording the one it ends; every verdict names its generation.
- **CI.** New step "Spike self-tests" ([`scripts/test-spikes.sh`](../scripts/test-spikes.sh)).
- **S2 harness.** The expiry two-writer audit required only one writer and one epoch, less than its README said. It now requires two of each.
- **Pairing size** ([D132](plan/decisions.md#d132), commit `0d4c09e`). The first full S2 run found that the 45th device could not pair: the welcome message grows by about 1.4 KB per device and had outgrown one encrypted message. A pairing message may now span several encrypted messages, at most 256 KiB in total, and a group holds at most 64 devices. The limit came in on 2026-11-07 and was unseen because every S2 run since used the quick mode with five test devices.

**Verification.** Four independent reviewers checked this round in up to three passes. The first pass found 3 high and 6 medium findings (a restarted node still stopped at a closed-epoch seal; the CloudKit route did not get a device out; a full inbox lost the arriving delivery); all were fixed and confirmed. The Mac and iOS app sources have no test target and were verified by build and by reading.

**Checks.** [`scripts/ci.sh`](../scripts/ci.sh) passed every step on that tree with **1,064 Swift tests and 68 release-tooling tests**, on the internal disk without `MEMOSHI_BUILD_ROOT` ([log](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-code-review/ci/after-d131-final.log)). A `--quick --load` S2 smoke passed with hand-over and expiry commit counts in the range of the recorded quick cohorts. These are development checks, not canonical evidence.

**Canonical evidence (2026-11-13), on commit `918cb25` with a clean tree** ([index of all the runs](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/README.md)). These remain the records of `918cb25`; later records are on commit `4db72e2` ([D133 section](#d133-work-2026-11-13)) on `f22517d` after D134, on `4658dcd`, and, current, on `7df84f9` ([pin move](#d134-pin-move-2026-11-14)):

- **Canonical CI:** unchanged `scripts/ci.sh` through the earlier runner passed all 17 steps: **1,073 Swift tests in 15 runs and 68 release-tooling tests**, 764.55 s, on a quiet machine ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-03/README.md)). An earlier pass on `67e750d` (1,064 tests) is kept as that commit's record, and one run that failed two 20-second timing tests under a load average of 212–487 is kept as invalid.
- **Full S2 rerun ([D123](plan/decisions.md#d123)):** `s2 --load --real-expiry` passed with `passed: true` and no failures; the independent load verifier and a separate check of the D123 points (36 of 36) passed ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-02/README.md)).

| Point | Measured |
|---|---|
| Revocation at the relay | The revoked device's raw hello was refused, and the relay counted the refusal |
| Revocation at the claim level | The raw claim got no answer on a closed connection; 0 grants to the revoked device afterwards |
| Revocation at the link level | The peer itself refused the raw link (`unknownPeer`); no link to the revoked device afterwards |
| Expiry two-writer audit | 172 commits, 2 writers, 40 epochs; no epoch with two writers |
| Expiry window | 20 cycles; the old runner was accepted 0 times and refused 6,518 times; 20 stale writes refused |
| Pairing-code expiry | A real wait of 301 s; the expired code was refused |
| Pairing | 51 probes paired; the largest group had 54 devices |

- **Caveats of that record.** At the claim level the refusal is the relay's: it holds no connection for a revoked device, so the authority itself was never asked, and the revoked iPad never held a lease in the run. The run on `4db72e2` removes this caveat: the authority itself refused the revoked device's claim and renewal, and the device had held a lease before its revocation ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-04/README.md)). The three "devices" are processes on one Mac over loopback; no physical iPhone or iPad and no CloudKit server took part. The load numbers are not deployed capacity.
- **The first full run failed and is kept** ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-01/README.md)): it led to D132.
- **Open finding, resolved by [D133](plan/decisions.md#d133).** With 54 devices hand-over took 637 ms at the median (12 ms in the 2026-11-03 record, which used an older wire and is not comparable). A group change costs about 40 ms with 44 devices against 10 ms with 9, and each message rewrites the saved group state, whose history grows to a cap of 16,384 entries, about 7–8 MB at 44 devices. Every other device makes two membership reads per message it receives; the committer's redundant second read per change was removed. D133 saves the group state as a journal: a message now writes about 21 KB with 8 devices and 31 KB with 54, against up to about 29 MB before, and the run on `4db72e2` measured hand-over p50 54 ms with 8 devices and 176 ms with 54 ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-04/README.md)). A group change still rewrites most of the native MLS state, about 1.4 MB at 54 devices; that is the remaining floor.

<a id="d133-work-2026-11-13"></a>
### Limits of the 2026-11-13 runs closed (D133)

**By the user's answer of 2026-11-13 ("do all that you can do"), recorded as [D133](plan/decisions.md#d133).** No product feature started. The code is in commits `cd440c4` (device layer), `58f2b01` (S2 harness and runtime tests) and `f12a6ff` (apps). Agents: Fable 5.2 agents did the first part and stopped on the provider's rate limit; the user chose to finish with Opus 5.5 agents. The re-check note for Fable is in [section 8](#8-next-moves).

- **A new device could not join a group that already had traffic.** Found by the quick S2 run once its new stress phase paired devices after traffic: the Mac's welcome message was not always its first message in the new group state, and a commit during pairing could use a membership answer read just before the add. Both causes predate this week (the new tests fail the same way on `d3e8d19`). Now nothing is sealed ahead of the welcome, a stale membership answer is ignored, and a send during an unfinished add waits or is refused with a retryable error while the device retries the add itself, with backoff. The 2026-11-18 review found that the send path retried only the host's completion: an add whose authority reply was lost stayed pending until `recover()`, `relayOnline()` or an adopted snapshot. Since `662ce10` the send path resolves that case too ([deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)).
- **Group state is saved as a journal.** Before, every message rewrote the whole state: about 29 MB per message once the history was full. Now a snapshot plus small change rows, compacted from time to time, with the consumed-message history in its own bounded file: about 21 KB per message with 8 devices and 31 KB with 54. Forward secrecy is kept: a group change is written as a full compaction, so replaced keys leave the disk when it returns; a per-message secret stays at most 64 further rows. Files from the committed code are converted once; there is no downgrade path. Writes are ordered with `F_BARRIERFSYNC`. The 2026-11-18 review found that a compaction's rename had no barrier, so a power loss could empty the CloudKit spool or the transport journal; since `662ce10` compaction writes behind a barrier and leaves a `.replaced` marker ([deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)).
- **A removed device.** After revocation the relay gives it only the group's membership changes, never a message of any epoch, and rechecks revocation at admission and delivery. The device verifies its own removal on a copy of its state and then deletes everything it received through the group, durably: group keys and its own signing key, history, replicas, the offline approval mailbox, the CloudKit spool and its saved re-key records, and the apps' conversation data. The apps close the session and say: "This device was removed from your devices. Data it received from them was deleted." A device only refused by the relay keeps its data. The 2026-11-18 review found that the transport store (`transport.jsonl`) was never erased and that the relay kept a removed device's mailbox rows; since `662ce10` both are erased or dropped, and the removed device's leases move to a new epoch at the removal ([protocol.md §12.5](specs/protocol.md#125-revocation)).
- **A full group** (64 devices, [D132](plan/decisions.md#d132)) issues no pairing code; a joiner that still gets through is told why and the code stays valid. The limit was enforced only on the device: the authority accepted a 65th, which then failed closed at the native roster. Since `662ce10` the authority refuses it too ([protocol.md §12.2](specs/protocol.md#122-pairing)).
- **S2 harness.** The revoked device holds a lease before it is revoked; its claim and renewal reach the relay and the authority, which refuse them. Group-size numbers are reported in a qualified phase (8 devices) and a separate stress phase.
- **Tested app logic.** The Mac model-key setup rules, the one automatic reload, the forward-only snapshot positions, the iPhone first-enrollment rules, the continued-processing decisions and the removal erase now live in `AppCore` with tests; the apps call them.
- **Smaller.** The Python runtime's retained licence texts are bundled in both apps. Two Code Mode timing tests no longer depend on start-up finishing inside the 20-second script limit. A dead result-bundle link is replaced by a note.

**Review.** Independent Opus reviewers checked each change, the removal path three times (one pass end to end with a kill at each step of the erase and a raw-byte search of every file). Their findings (one high, the relay sending a revoked device old messages; several medium; the rest low) are fixed and re-checked. The Mac and iOS app sources still have no test target.

**Checks.** [`scripts/ci.sh`](../scripts/ci.sh) passed every step on that tree with **1,225 Swift tests and 72 release-tooling tests**, on the internal disk without `MEMOSHI_BUILD_ROOT` ([log](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-code-review/ci/after-d133-final.log)). A development check, not a canonical run.

**Canonical records of this round**, on commit `4db72e2` (they remain that commit's records; the current ones are on `7df84f9`, [pin move](#d134-pin-move-2026-11-14)), which adds one fix: the device's hello is sealed and published under the send gate ([index](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/README.md)). The tree had no tracked change; the only line of `git status --short` was an untracked document from another session.

- **Canonical CI:** all 17 steps passed with **1,230 Swift tests in 15 runs and 72 release-tooling tests** ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-05/README.md)).
- **Full S2** (`s2 --load --real-expiry`): passed with no failures; the load verifier passed, the D123 check 60 of 60, and the extra check `recovery.unacceptedOldEpochReported` 0 ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-04/README.md)). That 60 of 60 is the checker of that time; on `s2-full-07` the review of 2026-11-18 found that it counts one always-true check and 13 checks that passed on counters missing from the report, and the fixed checker gives 38 of 76 there. The same applies to this record [INFERENCE: same checker and report layout] ([deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)).
- **Revocation:** refused at the relay, the authority, the claim, the renewal and the link, 1 each, none accepted. The revoked device held a lease before its revocation (1 grant, 3 commits, 1 renewal); after it, 0 of 2,702 grants and 0 writes (3 refused). This removes the earlier caveat that the authority was never asked and the revoked device never held a lease. The relay's refusals were not tied to each probe, so the claim refusal was not shown to be separate from the relay's refusal of the hello (review finding HT-07 of 2026-11-18; the driver now ties each refusal to its probe).
- **Expiry two-writer audit:** 1,774 commits in the qualified phase and 1,068 in the stress phase, each with 2 writers and 40 epochs; no epoch had two writers. The real pairing-code expiry was refused.
- **Group size:** qualified phase 8 devices, hand-over p50 54 ms (p95 463 ms), 2,142 accepted writes; stress phase 54 devices, p50 176 ms (p95 272 ms), 1,715 accepted writes. 5 and 45 probes paired, 0 unexpected refusals.
- **Hand-over finding resolved.** The earlier run's p50 637 ms at 54 devices came with the group state rewritten for every message, up to about 29 MB. With the journal a message writes about 21 KB with 8 devices and 31 KB with 54. A group change still rewrites most of the native MLS state, about 1.4 MB at 54 devices; that is the remaining floor.
- **Earlier records stay.** `canonical-ci-04` and `s2-full-03` remain the records of `3b503de` (`s2-full-03` reported `recovery.unacceptedOldEpochReported` 10, which `4db72e2` fixes); `canonical-ci-03` and `s2-full-02` remain those of `918cb25`.
- **Not covered by any run:** physical iPhone and iPad, CloudKit against Apple's servers and a deployed relay. Loopback numbers are not deployed capacity. The Mac and iOS app sources have no test target.

<a id="deep-review-2026-11-18"></a>
### Deep review and fixes (2026-11-18)

**Scope.** Every commit in `c119597~1..754cca2` (57 commits, 2026-11-13 to 2026-11-16; `2e67f65`, `3016ac1` and `754cca2` were local only) and the uncommitted working tree as it stood on 2026-11-18 (three iPhone landscape files from 2026-11-17). Eight read-only reviews, one per slice, then one fix agent per slice, test-first, every build and test run under one build lock. Every agent ran on Claude Opus 5.5, the session's model, so this is **not** the independent Fable review that [§8](#8-next-moves) still owes. The eight review reports are retained in [`2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/reviews/`](../../evidence/2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/reviews/); the fix agents' reports are summarised below. The fixes are committed in `754cca2..8b9b170`, one commit per slice: `782caec` (sandbox and egress), `ec23475` (store and runtime), `3f9aa47` (daemon and client), `662ce10` (device layer), `89ca9f4` (browser), `9398f76` (apps) and `8b9b170` (harnesses, tooling, scripts and spikes); these documents follow in the next commit. No product feature started, and the gate stays No-go.

**Findings by slice and severity.** Each review counted defects introduced in the range; older ones are listed as spec gaps in its report.

| Slice | Blocker | High | Medium | Low |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Apps (not the browser): AppCore, Shell/Conversation/DesignSystem, Mac and iPhone apps | 0 | 0 | 4 | 4 |
| Local IPC transport, daemon, client and protocol | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1 |
| Store and runtime adapter | 0 | 0 | 3 | 4 |
| This Mac sandbox, working copies, attribution and egress | 0 | 1 | 6 | 6 |
| WebKit bot browser (D136, site rule, form gate, runner waits) | 0 | 0 | 4 | 5 |
| Qualification harnesses and tooling | 1 | 2 | 11 | 9 |
| Device layer | 0 | 0 | 3 | 8 |
| Documents and evidence against the code | 0 | 2 | 10 | 6 |
| **Total** | **1** | **5** | **42** | **43** |

The blocker: `S4MatrixVectorTests` called an `internal` initializer through a non-`@testable` import, so the MemoshiDaemon test target could not compile, and no canonical run had built it. The highs: a detached command child could make a sandbox session's close hang forever; `memoshi-qualification s4-matrix` failed in its default form and its CLI path had never run; the `subagent` kill point's pass criteria never looked at the child; the [retired 2026-11-16 handoff](#session-2026-11-16) contradicted the gate draft, the evidence and the code; and the [E21 note](research/2026-11-16-e21-discovery-tools-policy.md) claimed argument rewrite was measured and had no working link.

**Fixed** (test-first: each new test failed before its fix and passed after, unless noted). The fix agents' test runs are not retained evidence; the canonical run that covers the fixes is below.
- **Store and runtime (7 of 7).** The durable dispatch commit now syncs the WAL with `synchronous = FULL` and then issues `F_FULLFSYNC` on the store's directory, as Pi_Swift's ADR-0001 does; a refused drain deletes the just-committed dispatch row and fails the dispatch, so the effect never starts. The PRAGMAs alone do not drain the device cache on Apple's SQLite (F1). A step Memoshi recorded as completed is never settled as not dispatched after Pi loses its result: it is held with a Resume card offering Skip and Mark as done (F2). Run again of a replay-unsafe call is admitted again under the policy and permission mode in force: where the policy asks, a new approval card opens for that execution, and a denied or expired one blocks the rerun (F3; [proposed decision](#proposed-decisions-2026-11-18)). `chats.abort` records the Stop durably (`task.cancel_requested`), so a stopped step never offers Run again, even after a later kill (F4). The model-text rewrite reads Pi's history in one bounded scan (F5; no failing-before run). A retried `cards.respond` hands the answer to Pi again (F6). A replay-safe step whose tool is no longer registered as replay-safe is held with a card (F7). New `SchemaEquivalenceTests`: stores upgraded from schema 1 and 12 equal a fresh schema 15. The resume latency guard fails on a phase median of 2 s or more, not on one slow reopen ([protocol.md](specs/protocol.md#current-step-settlement)).
- **IPC transport, daemon and client (2 of 2).** Refusals of mutating requests and of `bots.grants.list` follow the events their request committed, and an oversized result is refused only after its events (F1). Stop/start race closes use 4503 or 4500, not 1001 (F2). Under backpressure the client drops UI-only frames oldest first; a durable event that still does not fit closes the carrier and reconnects from the acknowledged cursor, with a delay doubling from 100 ms to the 5 s cap, instead of failing for good. `system.cancel` holds no in-flight slot. The host-call deadline test no longer waits 10 s ([protocol.md](specs/protocol.md)).
- **This Mac sandbox (13 of 13; F11's real-volume case not run).** Close ends the commands' process groups and waits at most 10 s, then closes the daemons, the gateway client and the log reader; a detached `setsid` child keeps running and is not reaped (F1). The guard refuses a path when the profile's own regular expressions or host admission's shared `SourcePathPolicy.namesSecret` would (scalar split, full case folding, default-ignorable characters dropped) (F2, F8, F9). Reports are parsed on bytes; every `(digits) deny` counts as a possible PID; a PID is kept only if it was in memoshid's tree; a truncated report is ignored only for live foreign processes older than the monitor (F3, F4, F13). A flood of truncated reports is one counted gap; monitor and Store state are bounded, and open gaps are never pruned (F5). Denials naming a purged chat are dropped, and `unknown_tag` denials keep no report text (F6). The command `cwd` is resolved lexically and entered by the confined shell, never looked up by the host (F7). Mask names the host owns are refused with `EgressConfigurationError.reservedSecretName` (F10). Export proves its deny ACL with a probe before any effect (F11). Toolchain roots travel as a JSON array (F12). The host-exec rule now matches the same D103 paths in any ASCII case ([S4 report](research/spikes/S4-this-mac-sandbox.md)).
- **WebKit bot browser (9 of 9).** A move's "from" site is the page a redirect leaves, the page a new-window link was on or the committed top-level document, never the initiating frame (F1). An unreadable site never matches, and a missing Public Suffix List refuses configuration (F2). Profile changes run through one sequencer that stops on close, revoke or reconfigure and rechecks after every `await` (F3). The `92ff0df` form gate could never take effect: the throwaway profile copies the bot's grants, and the network boundary refuses an ungranted origin before any form logic. It is removed, and the navigation decision is one tested AppCore function (F4). F5–F9 are fixed in the Mac app only, with harness tasks not yet run in a browser. Follow-up: a commanded open uses the bot's own profile only for a site, scheme included, whose pages that profile has shown; IP addresses have no registrable domain; the iPhone uses the AppCore resume gate ([S3 report](research/spikes/S3-webkit-browser.md)).
- **Harnesses and tooling (22 of 23; HT-23 in part).** `S4MatrixVectorTests` compiles (HT-01). `memoshi-qualification s4-matrix` runs in its default form; its first two CLI runs gave 72/25/3/5 and exit 2, on the frozen profile snapshot and on the re-snapshotted shipping profile, with every case's outcome the same ([run-1](../../evidence/2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/s4-matrix/run-1/README.md), [run-2](../../evidence/2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/s4-matrix/run-2/README.md)); `SandboxProfileDriftTests` requires the shipping profile to equal the snapshot; other volumes run with `--owned-root`; `--output-dir` must not exist (HT-02, HT-15–HT-17). The `subagent` kill point now checks the child, which the 2026-11-16 results (3 of 3, 2 of 2, 6 of 6) never did, and the default point set is the five gate points again, so the recorded S1.13 command reproduces its plan (HT-03–HT-05, HT-18, HT-19). The S2 checkers fail closed on absent counters; each relay refusal is tied to its probe; only fence or membership refusals count as refused writes, in `commitStream` and `writeLoop`; once-only scenarios report in their own `once` section (HT-06–HT-09, HT-20, HT-21). The pin check sees URL packages, the boundary lint fails on grep errors and on zero files, and TestFlight's terminal states end the wait (HT-10–HT-12). S3 harness: the `_blank` rows are gated to one empty preconnect, a probe must run to count, the lockdown suite needs a successful navigation, and T10's crash child is killed by SIGKILL with a per-run nonce (HT-13, HT-14, HT-22). New `scripts/check-bundle.sh --release-composition` scans Release products for the Debug-only tools; `release.py` runs it on the Mac app (verify and archive) and the iPhone app ([qualification README](../Packages/MemoshiDaemon/Sources/MemoshiQualification/README.md)).
- **Device layer (11 of 11, and four spec gaps the fixes found).** Compaction writes behind a barrier, renames on the held directory descriptor and leaves a `.replaced` marker; a file with that marker and no complete row refuses to open instead of opening empty (F1). A joiner accepts the inviter's hello opened in the add's epoch or later; the inviter re-seals an owed hello and reports success once the authority accepted the add, or "completion pending" when its host's completion of the add failed, which the node finishes on its retry (F2; this differs from D131, [proposed decision](#proposed-decisions-2026-11-18)). The transport store is erased after a verified removal, at `<zone>/transport` (F3). A seal's own receipt ends at the seal (F4); seals are serialized per message ID (F5); a refused later fragment closes the link (F6); `eraseAfterRemoval` tries every store and reports every failure (F7); `connectRelay` refuses after a removal (F8); the relay drops a removed device's mailbox (F9); an interrupted first enrollment's files are removed (F10); `reportRemoval` cannot return before the deletion (F11). The spec gaps, against D132, D133 and protocol.md §12.5: the authority now refuses a 65th member; the send path recovers an add whose authority reply was lost; a removal moves the removed device's leases to a new epoch with `reason: revoked`; and pairing a removed device again as a new device is not built (the host keeps its identity; [proposed decision](#proposed-decisions-2026-11-18)). Also: `OfflineApprovalMailbox.erase()` can be retried, and the device tests share one 30 s wait bound ([protocol.md §12](specs/protocol.md#12-device-layer), [S2 report](research/spikes/S2-devices.md)).
- **Apps (8 of 8, then a second round on the open items).** The iPhone landscape header keeps the preview-bot chooser and its Preview/Development disclosure, and Deny and Allow once sit side by side where they fit (F1–F3). Model setup has one credential remover: an API key is removed from the Keychain and a ChatGPT sign-in by sign-out; a credential that cannot be removed yet is kept and retried at the next confirmation, also across a relaunch (F4, F5, F7). Resume answers are cancelled by `detach()` and `stop()` (F6). The enrollment-rollback messages are localized (F8). Also: one AppCore Send-failure rule for both chat models (D133); the iPhone offers the unreadable-record discard only once the Chat's snapshot is loaded, as the Mac does (D130); `DevelopmentComposition` reads only launch arguments; a full group advertises no pairing over Bonjour. Second round: the saved Send of both apps lives in AppCore's `SavedSend`, which also keeps the latest refusal (`text-chat.pending-send-refusal`), so after a relaunch a refused Send still offers Discard, and a new request, a retry or a removal clears the reason (D130); `attach` clears the previous engine's providers, models and configuring state, so a re-attached engine lists its own models; a failed iPhone event subscription is retried for as long as the chat is attached, waiting 250 ms longer per consecutive failure up to 5 s, and `detach` cancels it; the remaining localization gaps are closed, and the catalog test, renamed `AppStringCatalogTests`, now also scans the Mac and iPhone app sources (tests in `InProcessChatModelRecoveryTests`, `SavedSendTests`, `AppStringCatalogTests`). No test target links ShellFeature, so the landscape changes were checked in the iPhone 14 Pro Simulator (iOS 27.0) at default and XXXL text. The first check found that the header no longer covers Allow once, but that right after rotating Allow once started out of view ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/landscape/README.md)). The second keeps the pending Card's buttons centred in view after a rotation, after a Card arrives and after the keyboard hides: Allow once was clear of the header and the composer in every landscape shot, with no drag, and at XXXL only its top 4 pt lay under the header bar's padding ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/landscape-2/README.md)).
- **Documents.** The audit's findings F1–F7 and F9–F18 are fixed in the documents; F8 (the unpushed commits and the failed `canonical-ci-13`) waits on the canonical run below. The 2026-11-16 handoff file is retired; its true facts are in [§8](#session-2026-11-16).

**Rejected or deferred, and why.**
- **Rejected:** reusing `BrowserGatewayTally` in the iPhone's `DevelopmentPythonTool`: it cannot start from a known count, and seeding it would copy the whole decision window per call. The tool reads `decisionWindow` once instead.
- **Waiting for the owner:** the [proposed decisions](#proposed-decisions-2026-11-18) below, and `git init` in the This Mac sandbox.
- **Transport:** the daemon still does not act on the app's `system.cancel`; a startup error swallowed when a stop wins is not logged, because the daemon has no logger. UI-frame merging in the daemon's send queue (§11), the signature check off the event loop and the DaemonSupervisor gaps were done on 2026-11-21; whether D130's 120 s also covers a kickstarted LaunchAgent is an owner question ([work of 2026-11-21](#work-2026-11-21)).
- **Runtime:** Pi stores still run at `.processKill`, so a power loss can still roll Pi back (now handled, F2); choosing `.powerLoss` is the host's decision under Pi_Swift's ADR-0001. Telling the model about a Run again that found no wait was done on 2026-11-21 ([work of 2026-11-21](#work-2026-11-21)). The export tool name was written in three places, and no test covered a graceful close followed by Run again or Skip; both were done on 2026-11-19 (`ac15844`, [work of 2026-11-19](#work-2026-11-19)).
- **Browser:** lab browsing has no task end, so a separate profile's lifetime is open-ended until someone decides what ends it (an owner question since 2026-11-21). Done on 2026-11-21 ([work of 2026-11-21](#work-2026-11-21)): the frame-embedding rule reads the private-data flag from the profile's store, and the injectable gateway gives BrowserD136Review F5 its harness task.
- **Sandbox:** detached `setsid` children survive close; reaping them by their launch environment was tried and dropped on 2026-11-19 (`2b85f74`, [work of 2026-11-19](#work-2026-11-19)). Pi_Swift request: `runCommand` should stop waiting on inherited pipes after a kill, abort or timeout once the leader has exited (today a tool call also runs past its own `timeout`). Done on 2026-11-21 ([work of 2026-11-21](#work-2026-11-21)): U+0600 reaches the reader unescaped, and a report cut inside a multi-byte character, which `log stream` delivers as a compose failure, is counted in the `report_truncated` gap; the window before the removal ACL is covered by a test; the guard decides the entries below an admitted listing.
- **Tooling:** HT-23's licence check by path rather than content, crate exceptions by name and AUTHORS-only evidence are [D126](plan/decisions.md#d126) policy calls. Done on 2026-11-21 ([work of 2026-11-21](#work-2026-11-21)): `build-memoshi-mls.sh` stages inside `Artifacts/`, verifies, and installs by same-volume renames, with the payload manifest removed before the first rename and rewritten after the last, so a stopped install leaves no manifest; older than the range, S3's fail-closed rows and sink liveness are measured, load relays without a grant log report no duplicate count, and a test shows S2's `refusingAuthority` splits renewals at the reported removal.
- **Device layer:** [D131](plan/decisions.md#d131)'s "new message IDs are always minted above the mark" holds only for `DeviceApplicationTransport.nextMessageID()`, which no app calls yet; a host that mints with `DeviceULID.next()` after the clock is set back is refused with `retiredMessageIdentifier`. Since 2026-11-21 `DeviceMessageIDSourceTests` fails if an app source calls `DeviceULID.next()` ([work of 2026-11-21](#work-2026-11-21)). Pairing a removed device again waits for the owner ([proposed decision](#proposed-decisions-2026-11-18)).
- **Apps:** in landscape the transcript gets only about 69 pt at default and 45 pt at XXXL between the header bar and the composer, Deny (58 pt) does not fit at XXXL, and with the keyboard up no transcript shows and the tab bar, the Development Review row and the Palette buttons are drawn over the composer; that needs a layout decision (on 2026-11-21, with the keyboard up, the tab bar still covered the composer and Palette the Send button, before and after that day's header change). The Development Review row shows in every build. Not run: accessibility text sizes (the 2026-11-20 and 2026-11-21 audits used Large and XXXL), devices, and Resume choices on rotation. Done on 2026-11-21 ([work of 2026-11-21](#work-2026-11-21)): re-attaching while a configuration runs waits and asks again; the Mac and iPhone/iPad app sources have test targets, though `LiveChatModel`'s reconnect and resumed-flag handling is not covered; a kept credential is reported, and its removal is retried each time Model Settings open.

**Verification.**

- **Working-tree check (development, not a canonical record).** Before the commits, unchanged [`scripts/ci.sh`](../scripts/ci.sh) ran on the working tree whose code equals `8b9b170` and passed all 17 steps in 920 s: 1,465 Swift Testing tests in 15 runs plus 6 XCTest tests, 105 release-tooling tests, the spike self-tests (66 + 2 Python tests), the Mac and iPhone/iPad Simulator builds, sign-in flows and bundle layout ([log](../../evidence/2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/ci-worktree/ci.txt)). The tree was not clean, so this is not a canonical record.
- **Canonical CI on the committed tree:** [`canonical-ci-15`](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-15/README.md) passed on `a2ac274` with a clean tree: all 17 steps in 951 s, 1,465 Swift Testing tests in 15 runs plus 6 XCTest tests, 105 release-tooling tests. [`canonical-ci-14`](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-14/README.md) on the same commit failed with three `PyodideRealmLifetimeTests` timeouts while another session's Pi_Swift test run took the 5-minute load to 44; it is kept as invalid. The current canonical CI record is canonical-ci-25 on `7f766c8` ([work of 2026-11-21](#work-2026-11-21)).
- **Full S2 run under the fixed driver and checker:** [`s2-full-08`](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-08/README.md) passed on `a2ac274`: report passed with no failures, qualified 8 devices and stress 54; revocation refused at all five points, with 0 of 2,703 grants and 0 writes to the revoked device after its revocation; hand-over p50 56.31 ms at 8 devices and 209.17 ms at 54. The fail-closed `check_d123_v2.py` passed 61 of 61 once its revocation check expected the lease the removal moves to the next epoch (`662ce10`, [protocol.md §12.5](specs/protocol.md#125-revocation)); that one-line checker change is in the records commit after `a2ac274`. `check_d133_extra.py` and the sample verifier passed.

The last canonical run before the fixes, `canonical-ci-13` on `754cca2`, **failed** in package tests: 21 issues, all in `BrowserNetworkBoundaryTests`, with Proton VPN and Tailscale network extensions active, and it never reached the later packages ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-13/README.md)). Other retained development evidence from this round: the two s4-matrix runs and the two landscape checks above.
