Its [§13](research/2026-11-12-memoshi-cognitio-bot-hq-merge.md#13-what-the-user-decided-2026-11-13) records what the user decided; its recommendation to freeze Cognitio's features was not taken (D107). |

These documents contain the primary-source links, inspected revisions and evidence limitations. [The research index](research/README.md) also points to the older landscape, long-tail survey and in-house audits; those remain useful for areas the refresh did not revisit.

#### Design and runtime constraints to carry forward

1. **One engine, one writer per conversation.** Pi_Swift runs in memoshid, a Swift LaunchAgent, on Mac and inside the iPhone/iPad app. Each Chat, Session or lane has its own store and epoch-fenced writer ([D44](plan/decisions.md#d44), [D46](plan/decisions.md#d46), [D74](plan/decisions.md#d74)); one bot may run different conversations on different devices. Routine/watch lanes own their claims, not Chat ([D83](plan/decisions.md#d83)). External workers are tools, not competing schedulers or permission engines. Model output, memory, fetched content and generated views do not grant permission.
2. **Live resources are not approval receipts.** Keep the one-shot decision Cards and `cards.respond`. [D53](plan/decisions.md#d53) adds native cards for Apple data and generated cards drawn from a block catalog, whose buttons pass policy like any other call. Actions bind to the reviewed payload/revision and revalidate at execution. See [native resource contract](plan/NEXT_VERSION.md#4-native-resource-views-the-missing-contract).
3. **Device identity matters.** Tools run where they live: every typed tool declares where it can run, a runner calls another device's tools over the device links, and the executing device re-checks its own grants ([D49](plan/decisions.md#d49), [D50](plan/decisions.md#d50)). Music, alarms, Health, Home and EventKit capabilities differ by platform; use the feasibility matrix, not screenshots, as the implementation guide.
4. **Recovery is a whole-thread concern** (observed in Pi 1.3.0; spike S1 re-checks it on Pi_Swift, [D46](plan/decisions.md#d46)). `submit`, `abort`, `compact` and wait calls can enable recovered work, not only `resume()`. Safe mode must not make progress calls. `beforeTool` is not rerun after recorded intent; the executor must revalidate grants. A thrown `afterTool` hook is not a fail-closed redaction strategy. See [runtime gates](plan/NEXT_VERSION.md#runtime-gates-revealed-by-the-refresh).
5. **External effects can be uncertain.** Keep [D42](plan/decisions.md#d42) and [D51](plan/decisions.md#d51): run the tool's outcome check, then ask with a Resume card, instead of claiming exactly-once execution or automatically repeating a write.
6. **Separate memory jobs.** Scoped SQLite/FTS5 knowledge, Pi conversation history, lossy context summaries and external app state are different stores/contracts. Memory is evidence, not authority. OptMem is not the proposed replacement; any compaction experiment must pass quality, cost, scope and deletion gates.
7. **Keep the host boundary.** [D41](plan/decisions.md#d41) still excludes host Accessibility/ScreenCaptureKit control in v1/v1.1, and [D56](plan/decisions.md#d56) rules out Full Disk Access. Native public APIs and guest computer control do not imply permission to automate the user's entire Mac. Cua background control's private APIs are not silently adopted. GUI computer use, in v1.0 since [D113](plan/decisions.md#d113), acts only inside a Bot computer or a Cloud computer.
8. **Selective reuse, with provenance.** [D33](plan/decisions.md#d33), amended by [D69](plan/decisions.md#d69) for iSH and [D91](plan/decisions.md#d91) for qualifying personal-use Kuvo Code reuse, governs licensing. D91 supersedes D55's source segregation and private-work legal gate. Imported material keeps its terms and provenance; third-party art/private captures are not implicitly authorized. Since [D106](plan/decisions.md#d106) that Kuvo reuse is not taken for the interface, which is written new, and permissive dependencies come from their original upstreams, not from Cognitio's forks. Fetched installer instructions remain data, never authority to execute them.

#### Continuation path

1. Read the comparison and proposal, then the accepted [MASTER_PLAN](plan/MASTER_PLAN.md) and [decisions](plan/decisions.md). Before editing, identify which requested work is already accepted and which is proposed; keep the distinction explicit.
2. For adoption work, use [NEXT_VERSION §12](plan/NEXT_VERSION.md#12-adoption-map-changes-versus-preserved-contracts) and [change control](plan/decisions.md#change-control). Complete the linked policy, protocol, event, object-model, platform and roadmap changes together. In particular, reconcile the existing architecture/bot docs' `beforeCompact` treatment and whole-file recovery rules before implementing the Pi adapter.
3. For implementation, start from [Phase 0](plan/phase-0.md) and the proposal's [dependency gates](plan/NEXT_VERSION.md#13-delivery-order-and-stop-conditions), subject to adopted scope. Gate A requires a real model, tools, persistence, policy, recovery and effect evidence—not a fake model plus a shell screenshot. Review the [in-house asset audit](research/agentic-workspace-landscape/landscape/in-house-assets.md) before selectively porting anything.
4. Treat [§14's scenarios](plan/NEXT_VERSION.md#14-what-would-prove-the-combination-is-better) as future acceptance work, not results already achieved. Report observed state and failures; “SOTA” is a goal to measure.

#### Verification and transfer limits

The research pass checked 648 local links/anchors across 13 new/updated Markdown files, all supplied URLs in the ledger, Markdown fences/tables, and the SVG schematic rendered in Chromium. It also observed public sites/media as recorded in the investigations. **None of that proves a Memoshi runtime, Apple permission/effect path, Pi recovery integration or competitor task benchmark.** See the report's [verification record](research/2026-10-20-comparison-report.md#13-verification-gaps-and-freshness).

Share this repository (or the complete `docs/` tree), not this file alone: its links are relative. No previous agent IDs, browser tabs or temporary files are required. External local assets listed in §9 are optional inspected references, not assumed to exist on the receiving agent's machine; their audits are included here. This handoff adds context only and changes no accepted product decision.

## 2. The product in five lines

- **What:** native Mac, iPhone and iPad apps where a personal **Assistant** runs a team of persistent **bots**, each with a name, face, job, memory, **computer** and **browser**, and the same bots on every device ([MASTER_PLAN §1](plan/MASTER_PLAN.md#1-the-product), [D47](plan/decisions.md#d47), [D49](plan/decisions.md#d49)). You talk to the Assistant and let it coordinate, or drive bots and coding sessions directly ([D109](plan/decisions.md#d109)). Bots also build **Apps** under policy ([D114](plan/decisions.md#d114)), work with your mail, calendar and other personal information ([D116](plan/decisions.md#d116)) and speak ([D111](plan/decisions.md#d111)); from Phase 2 a bot may have a full computer of its own, a Bot computer or a Cloud computer ([D113](plan/decisions.md#d113)).
- **How:** the engine is **Pi_Swift**, the Swift port of Pi on the latest upstream. On the Mac, a SwiftUI/AppKit app (macOS 27+, Apple silicon) talks to **memoshid**, a Swift LaunchAgent running Pi_Swift; on iPhone and iPad (iOS and iPadOS 27+), Pi_Swift runs inside the app. Devices link directly, through our relay or through the user's iCloud ([D44](plan/decisions.md#d44), [D46](plan/decisions.md#d46), [D48](plan/decisions.md#d48), [D56](plan/decisions.md#d56)).
- **Look:**
  - Cognitio's design leads the shell on Mac, iPad and iPhone, rebuilt in Memoshi's own code as one SwiftUI codebase; Viticci's grammar stays for native Apple-data cards, Apps and phone navigation ([D106](plan/decisions.md#d106)). No Kuvo- or Cognitio-derived source file, asset or string enters this repository, so the reuse D91 permits is not taken for the interface. The earlier Viticci-style preview is frozen at git tag `design-preview-2026-11-13`; Memoshi's own tokens and avatar set are reused where they fit. Surfaces are reviewed through Memoshi scenarios rather than competitor-pixel identity. Cognitio keeps developing in parallel as the behaviour reference and the oracle for ports ([D107](plan/decisions.md#d107)).
  - Later design passes, once the system is stable: Aiden's motion (pinned to 0.36.1), a Grok-style roster with livelier avatars of our own, and optional Metal shader effects ([03 backlog](plan/03-design-system.md#design-pass-backlog)); generated motion and shader effects and native 3D and Gaussian-splat views are later passes too ([D115](plan/decisions.md#d115)).
- **Safety:** one policy engine outside the model; tiers T0–T5; a permission mode per bot (Ask first by default, Auto or Bypass); drafts for anything external outside Bypass; isolation per bot by default ([D9](plan/decisions.md#d9), [D16](plan/decisions.md#d16), [D27](plan/decisions.md#d27), [D52](plan/decisions.md#d52)).
- **Why it can win:**
  - one assistant that runs a team, and a team you can drive yourself;
  - isolation by default;
  - governed memory;
  - cost you see before you spend;
  - native and local;
  - a real workbench.

  These are the gaps the [landscape research](research/agentic-workspace-landscape/README.md#6-white-space-memoshi-can-own) found.

## 3. The user's decisions (2026-10-20) and how the plan implements them

| The user asked for | The plan |
|---|---|
| A native Swift app like Kuvo Code | One SwiftUI codebase for Mac, iPad and iPhone, with AppKit or UIKit only where a surface needs it. Cognitio, the user's Kuvo-derived Mac app, leads the design of the shell and is rebuilt in Memoshi's own code; Viticci's grammar stays for native Apple-data cards, Apps and phone navigation ([D106](plan/decisions.md#d106), which supersedes D92's hierarchy). D91 still permits source inspection, but no Kuvo- or Cognitio-derived code enters the interface, and no fork is selected. Pi_Swift remains the engine. |
| Latest Pi: Durable, Code Mode, MCP | Pi_Swift, the Swift port of Pi, always on the latest upstream `main`, behind our adapter, with contract tests on every update. Code Mode and MCP deferred tools; computers as Pi `env`s ([D44](plan/decisions.md#d44), [D6](plan/decisions.md#d6)–[D9](plan/decisions.md#d9)) |
| Aiden Agent's design and animations | A later design pass, once the system is stable: Aiden 0.36.1 motion for streaming, thinking, tool calls and cards, each with a Reduce Motion form and a frame budget, plus optional Metal shader effects ([03 backlog](plan/03-design-system.md#design-pass-backlog)) |
| Bots like Grok Bot | Persistent bots with avatar states, Chats, typed Cards, the Inbox, routines, wakes and watches; Handoffs; bot-to-bot messages in Phase 1, and Groups with a lead and Goals in Phase 2 ([05-bots-and-assistant.md](plan/05-bots-and-assistant.md), [D109](plan/decisions.md#d109)). The Grok-style roster look and livelier avatars come in a design pass ([03 §10.9](plan/03-design-system.md#livelier-avatars)) |
| A browser and a computer for each bot | Every Mac bot has **This Mac (sandboxed)** and a WebKit profile from Phase 1; on iPhone and iPad, bots get the in-app environment and a WebKit profile in Phase 3, plus an optional emulated Linux computer through iSH ([D69](plan/decisions.md#d69)). **Bot computer** VMs with Chromium arrive at the end of Phase 2 and **Cloud computers** after them, behind the same seam and chosen per bot; **Remote machines** arrive in Phase 3 ([06-computers-and-browsers.md](plan/06-computers-and-browsers.md), [D113](plan/decisions.md#d113)) |
| Bots can code through Pi | Projects and Sessions with Pi coding tools in the bot's computer, the Changes review loop and worktrees (Phase 2). External coding agents (Codex app-server, Claude Code, ACP), on the Mac only, and an optional team mode per project also come in Phase 2 ([07-coding.md](plan/07-coding.md), [D110](plan/decisions.md#d110)) |
| Personal assistant | The **Assistant**: pinned, owns the user profile memory, does small things itself and hands the rest off through visible Handoffs from Phase 1, triages the Inbox, writes the daily brief ([D20](plan/decisions.md#d20), [D109](plan/decisions.md#d109)). The `mail.*` tools and the built-in panes for Mail, Calendar, Reminders merged with Tasks, Contacts, Notes, and Files with the Library are on the Mac in Phase 1 and on iPhone and iPad in Phase 3 ([D116](plan/decisions.md#d116)) |

## 4. Plan at a glance

| Phase | Weeks | Dates | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 Foundations and spikes | 5 | 2026-10-26 → 2026-11-27 | Repo, app and Swift daemon skeleton, protocol, Pi_Swift adapter on a real model, design primitives; spikes S1 (the Pi_Swift gate on macOS and iOS), S2 (devices), S3 (WebKit on Mac and iOS), S4 (This Mac sandbox without Node), S5 (ship path), S6 (Pyodide on iPhone, first on the cut list) |
| 1 The Mac: the Assistant, bots, Apps and personal information | 46 | 2026-11-30 → **2027-10-29 (M1)** (break 2026-12-21 → 2027-01-01) | The shell rebuilt on Cognitio's design; Assistant, roster, Chats, streamed catalog cards, Inbox and a global Tasks view, single-bot Handoffs, bot-to-bot messages, policy with Ask first, Auto and Bypass, memory, routines, wakes, watches, budget floor, MCP and the connector catalog, `web_search` and `x_search`, WebKit browser, This Mac sandbox, the App contract with page Apps, the `mail.*` tools and the six built-in panes, Claude Code as a model, onboarding |
| 2 The Mac: the workbench, teams, voice and bot computers | 83 | 2027-11-01 → **2029-06-29 (M2)** (breaks 2027-12-20 → 2027-12-31 and 2028-12-25 → 2029-01-05 [INFERENCE]) | Projects, Sessions, coding tools in the sandbox, Changes review, terminal, worktrees, commit/PR, bots on projects; external coding agents, team mode and Attempts; Groups and Goals; the Swift engine's foundations, with live Swift cards and Swift Apps on the Mac, opened by the Swift engine probe (W52); plugin developer mode; voice on the Mac; connector events, Composio, image and video generation; Python and notebooks on the Mac; at the end, Bot computers, then Cloud computers, with GUI computer use |
| 3 iPhone, iPad and devices | 55 | 2029-07-02 → 2030-08-02 (Phase 3 gate; break 2029-12-24 → 2030-01-04 [INFERENCE]) | The iPhone and iPad app with Pi_Swift inside, pairing, direct, relay and iCloud links, bots on every device, typed tools with a shell front end, push and approvals on every device, budgets and cost preview; Python through Pyodide and notebooks on iPhone and iPad; the Swift engine, Swift Apps, App panes, the `mail.*` tools, the built-in panes and voice there; Remote machines and the always-on Mac host; watching and taking over a bot's browser or computer from iPhone and iPad; the iSH computer, opened by the iSH probe (W135) |
| 4 Hardening and v1.0 | 8 | 2030-08-05 → **2030-09-27 (M3, the v1.0 gate)** | Performance, security review (mail, Apps, voice and bot computers included), accessibility audit, signed Mac releases and internal TestFlight builds on the user's own devices; no App Review or store release ([D67](plan/decisions.md#d67)) |
| D1 Drop 1: App power | 10 | 2030-09-30 → 2030-12-06 | App background jobs and widgets on Mac, iPhone and iPad, the signed bytecode cache, the rest of Swift, rich styling, the adaptive kit ([D87](plan/decisions.md#d87)); the bot's digest of program actions moved to Phase 2 ([D114](plan/decisions.md#d114)) |
| D2 Drop 2: devices and polish | 9 | 2030-12-09 → 2031-02-21 (break 2030-12-23 → 2031-01-03 [INFERENCE]) | The open model on the phone, browser sign-ins across devices, coding on iPhone and iPad, notebook extras, live Apple-data cards, the visual task and artifact views, live QuickAsk drafts, the Apple tools ([D87](plan/decisions.md#d87)); takeover across devices moved to Phase 3 ([D113](plan/decisions.md#d113)) |
| 5 v1.1: reach | — | Sized at the v1.0 gate (from 2031-02-24) | Shared rooms, Telegram, teach-by-demonstration, public webhooks through the relay; the first design pass by default |
| 6 v2 | — | Sized at the Phase 5 gate | Team bots, bot identities, more channels, sharing, the hosted offer |

Dates come from [10-roadmap.md](plan/10-roadmap.md#calendar) and [D122](plan/decisions.md#d122), which supersedes [D87](plan/decisions.md#d87)'s calendar: 197 plan weeks to M3, the v1.0 gate, and 19 more for the two drops, with the year-end breaks outside them. Every duration and date after Phase 0 is an [INFERENCE] estimate for one builder with coding agents, and a gate review may re-date a later phase by a note. M1, M2 and M3 name the gates at the ends of Phases 1, 2 and 4; each milestone is usable on its own, and nothing left v1.0 ([D117](plan/decisions.md#d117)). One builder directs coding agents, with user design review and optional gap-specific outside help—not a mandatory contract ([D45](plan/decisions.md#d45), [D93](plan/decisions.md#d93)). Cognitio continues beside Memoshi in its own repository, so two live codebases share that builder's attention ([D107](plan/decisions.md#d107)). Level 1 cuts apply from the start. **Phase 1 carries the most catalog rows:** 177 of the 345 v1.0 rows; Phase 2 has 101 and is the longest phase, at 83 plan weeks; Drop 1 has 6 and Drop 2 has 8 ([feature-catalog](plan/feature-catalog.md#summary)). The [cut order](plan/10-roadmap.md#cut-order) is pre-agreed.

## 5. Corrections to the previous handoff

The previous handoff was accurate on most points: paths, installed apps, Pi package facts, plan-pack decisions and research summaries. These items were wrong, stale or missing, and the plan now reflects the corrections.

| Previous handoff said | What is true (checked 2026-10-20) |
|---|---|
| `~/Documents/Memoshi-Plan` is "planning docs only" | It also holds a three-commit build: a SwiftUI app plus a Node sidecar on pi-durable 1.3.0. Its runtime only ever ran Pi's fake model, and it has real defects; for example, its seeded agent can overwrite project files without approval. Port selectively ([audit](research/agentic-workspace-landscape/landscape/in-house-assets.md#draft-3-build), [D36](plan/decisions.md#d36)). Since 2026-10-22 it continues as a separate prototype with its own agent, which does not bind Memoshi ([D65](plan/decisions.md#d65)) |
| Read docs at commit `181a939` as the "pre-build" version | `181a939` is the first build commit ("Phase 0 foundation and Phase 1 workbench"). It holds the earliest doc versions, not a pre-build state |
| Aiden has "up to four read-only subagents" | True only of the old local checkout (0.36.1). Current Aiden (0.58.0) adds an implementer that can write and run shell commands within the parent's permissions |
| Aiden ported its motion to Rust/GPUI "with a complete motion parity audit" | The port exists (branch `gpui-rust`), but its own audit marks parity incomplete. Use it as a spec with gaps |
| Apollo runs Pi Durable 1.1 | Apollo now pins pi-durable 1.3.0. The 2026-10-18 research read an older checkout at Pi 0.74.0 |
| "Pi has no permission system" | Pi ships no policy, but it has blocking hooks: `beforeToolCall` in pi-agent-core, and `beforeTool`/`afterTool` in Pi Durable, which also fire for calls made from Code Mode scripts ([harnesses](research/agentic-workspace-landscape/landscape/agent-harnesses.md#pi)) |
| MCP tools are namespaced `mcp__server__tool` | That is a convention in Pi's examples, not something Pi enforces |
| Draft 3 put VMs and computer use in v2 | Draft 3 put VMs in Phase 4 (or 5) and computer use in v2. The 2026-10-20 plan put Bot computer VMs in Phase 3 of v1; [D47](plan/decisions.md#d47) moved them to v1.1 on 2026-10-22; [D113](plan/decisions.md#d113) moved them back into v1.0 on 2026-11-13, at the end of Phase 2, with GUI computer use inside them |
| Foundation Models only for titles and summaries | Draft 3 used it for more: memory proposals, commit messages, tool summaries and risk explanations. Draft 3 also contradicted itself on a cloud fallback |
| Grok card types (purchase, Secure Form, sign-in) come from the MemoBot spec | Those come from our Grok Bot profile, which cites the changelog. MemoBot measured Grok Bot 0.71.2; the installed app is now **0.72.0** |
| `pi-durable-components` has scheduling, UI, browser and voice extensions | Its scheduling ("Pulses"), UI SDK and voice parts are local-only and absent from its published 0.6.0; MIT, 5 stars |
| `Kuvo.app` 0.1.3: role not researched | It is **Kuvo Box**, a native Docker-in-a-VM app (PolyForm Noncommercial). OpenBot 0.7.0 and Rakazo 0.2.0 are also installed, far behind their latest releases (0.44.3 and 0.3.1) |
| macOS 27+ because of "SwiftUI 27 APIs" | Liquid Glass and Foundation Models are macOS 26. macOS 27 adds conveniences, and Draft 3 was wrong about two "SwiftUI 27" features. The floor stays at 27 with revisit triggers ([D31](plan/decisions.md#d31)) |
| (missing) | **Gondolin** (`@earendil-works/gondolin`, Apache-2.0) is Pi's maker's own micro-VM sandbox, with egress allowlists, secret placeholders and a Pi extension. Its egress and secret model is adopted ([D17](plan/decisions.md#d17)) |
| (missing) | `pi-server`, `pi-client` and `pi-protocol` stopped at 1.1.0 and are gone from Pi's repo. The app↔daemon protocol is ours ([protocol](specs/protocol.md)) |
| (missing) | Node 22 reaches end of life on 2027-04-30. The plan pinned Node 24.22.0 and planned Node 26 LTS if spike S5 passed ([D15](plan/decisions.md#d15)). Superseded on 2026-10-22: no Node ships unless the Mac misses the Pi_Swift gate ([D46](plan/decisions.md#d46)) |

**Withdrawn:** an earlier version of this table said the "no web view" line came only from Kuvo Box. That was wrong. kuvo.run/code says it of Kuvo Code too: "SwiftUI and AppKit with Liquid Glass. No Electron, and no web view for the interface."

### 5.1 The Kuvo clean-build addendum (checked 2026-10-20)

The historical `~/Documents/Memoshi-Plan/HANDOFF-KUVO-CLEAN-BUILD.md` recorded a clean build and Kuvo's “100/100” look as the starting point, preserved in [D43](plan/decisions.md#d43). [D91](plan/decisions.md#d91) subsequently allows qualifying source inspection/reuse, and [D92](plan/decisions.md#d92) replaces blanket visual parity with the assistant/workbench hierarchy. [D106](plan/decisions.md#d106) (2026-11-13) then makes Cognitio's design the lead for the shell, rebuilt in Memoshi's own code, and takes no Kuvo-derived code for the interface. The staged Aiden motion, Grok-style avatars and optional shaders still wait for their later design passes.

These facts in it hold. Each is stated in Kuvo's public files or on kuvo.run:
- the quoted licence clauses (verbatim), the `NOTICE`, and the README and FAQ wording;
- the telemetry default, as the FAQ and privacy docs state it;
- the third-party library names and versions, as `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.txt` lists them;
- the Noodle, Lorca, Aiden, Rakazo and OpenMausBot facts.

These points changed when adopting it:

| The addendum says | What holds |
|---|---|
| Layout, sizes, spacing and density "can be identical" | D92 no longer requires exact competitor parity. Design/art rights remain a separate risk ([R37](plan/risks.md#r37)); D91 permits qualifying source/reuse work, not every asset or future public/commercial release. The pre-public-release rights review remains. Since [D106](plan/decisions.md#d106) the shell follows Cognitio's design and is built from written behaviour descriptions, not copied. |
| The observer studies the running app and may read Kuvo's source | D55 prohibited source access on 2026-10-22; D91 supersedes that rule on 2026-10-23. The user, observers and builders may read source and adapt code for qualifying personal/noncommercial use with terms, notices and provenance. No mandatory observer/builder separation remains. [D106](plan/decisions.md#d106) keeps the reading and drops the adapting for the interface: Cognitio's running app and source are read as a behaviour reference, and no Kuvo- or Cognitio-derived source file, asset or string enters this repository. |
| Track A, a personal fork, is optional and kept separate | D86 dropped it on 2026-10-22; D91 makes a personal fork an allowed option again on 2026-10-23. [D106](plan/decisions.md#d106) settles it for the interface on 2026-11-13: no fork and no imported frontend; the interface is written new. |
| Kuvo's MIT libraries: SwiftTerm, Textual, Prism, Sparkle, KeyboardShortcuts, OpenUsage, browser-use, opencode | Kuvo's `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.txt` also lists swift-concurrency-extras 1.4.1 (MIT) and swiftui-math 0.1.0 (MIT, with fonts under SIL OFL 1.1 and the GUST Font License). Sparkle's bundled parts include BSD and zlib code. Take each library from upstream, never from a Kuvo build or from Cognitio's forks ([D106](plan/decisions.md#d106)) |
| Kuvo shipped "0.1.0 on Oct 13, 0.5.22 a week later" | 29 releases in six days: v0.1.0 on 2026-10-13 and v0.5.22 on 2026-10-19 |
| Noodle's bots are vendor command-line agents | Mostly. Noodle also runs its own agent loop for Apple Foundation Models, MLX and OpenAI-compatible models |
| References to "D11" and "Q6" | Those are Draft 3 numbers. Here they are [D33](plan/decisions.md#d33) (open core) and [D40](plan/decisions.md#d40)/O1 (business model) |
| Reuse table: Noodle, Aiden, Rakazo, OpenMausBot core | Matches [MASTER_PLAN §13](plan/MASTER_PLAN.md#13-what-we-reuse). OpenMausBot's `enterprise/` folder is source-available and excluded |

## 8. Next moves

**Order of work after the 2026-11-13 decisions:** nothing below changes before the Phase 0 gate on Fri 2026-11-27. Finish Phase 0's tickets, spikes and gate evidence as listed; D106–D122 add no product feature before that gate, and no Phase 1 feature starts early. After the gate, Phase 1 opens in W6 (Mon 2026-11-30) in the order [10-roadmap.md](plan/10-roadmap.md#phase-1) sets. The transcript comes first, built and measured before the other surfaces ([amendment §9](plan/AMENDMENT_2026-11-13.md#9-risks-added-or-raised)); then the conversation's states (Cards, approvals, permission modes, the composer's queue, steer and stop); then the shell (card window, sidebar, side panel, tabs, split view), the roster and the This Mac sandbox. That is internal milestone M1.1, when the builder starts daily use. Each surface gets a written behaviour description before it is built ([D106](plan/decisions.md#d106)). The later blocks follow in this order: streamed catalog cards with native diagrams, charts and math; memory, routines, wakes, watches, the Inbox, the budget floor and Claude Code as a model (M1.2, the dogfood build); Handoffs, bot-to-bot messages, connectors, search and the WebKit browser (M1.3); the App contract and page Apps (M1.4); the `mail.*` tools and the Mail pane, then the Calendar, Reminders and Contacts tools and the other built-in panes (M1.5); and onboarding, before the M1 gate on Fri 2027-10-29. Only the roadmap sets the internal milestones' weeks.

<a id="d134-pin-move-2026-11-14"></a>
**Pin move of 2026-11-14 ([D134](plan/decisions.md#d134)):**
- **Current pin.** Memoshi builds against Pi_Swift's tag `slice-2` (commit `ce1728ef8236d95a8b94b50a856670c48ad85365`, tagged 2026-11-12; Pi_Swift tagged `slice-1` the same day). The pin is named once, in [`scripts/lib/pi-swift-pin.sh`](../scripts/lib/pi-swift-pin.sh). `Packages/MemoshiCore/Package.swift` uses the clean clone `../Pi_Swift-slice-2` by path, because SwiftPM refuses a revision pin on that commit (its manifest has a local package). [`scripts/check-prerequisites.sh`](../scripts/check-prerequisites.sh) creates that clone from the sibling `../Pi_Swift` and refuses to build unless it is exactly that commit, with a clean tree and the `slice-2` tag on it. The pin no longer appears in the `Package.resolved` files. The old candidate `892585fc8d8be3093b58c34aacffcd4e68c3a8d9` is history; the dated sections above name it as the pin of their time.
- **Gate evidence.** From this pin on, S1 results count for the gate ([D90](plan/decisions.md#d90)). Results recorded on the candidate, including the 2026-11-14 kill batches, stay development evidence. The canonical CI run and the full S2 run were repeated on `f22517d`, the commit that holds the move; these remain that commit's records, and the current ones are on `a2ac274` (below) ([index](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/README.md)). CI: 17 of 17 steps, **1,242 Swift tests in 15 runs and 74 release-tooling tests** ([canonical-ci-06](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-06/README.md)). S2 (`s2 --load --real-expiry`, which does not depend on Pi): passed with no failures; the load verifier passed, the D123 check 60 of 60 (the checker of that time, which the 2026-11-18 review found counts one always-true check and checks that pass on missing counters, [deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)); revocation refused at all five points (relay, authority, claim, renewal and link; the relay's refusals not tied to each probe), with 0 grants and 0 writes after revocation; hand-over p50 62.58 ms with 8 devices and 208.41 ms with 54 ([s2-full-05](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-05/README.md)). The tree had no tracked change; the only line of `git status --short` was an untracked document from another session. The records on `4db72e2` and earlier commits remain the records of those commits.
- **Interrupted steps** ([D138](plan/decisions.md#d138)). Memoshi now uses the engine call that settles an interrupted step (`harness.settle` with Run again, Skip or Mark as done; Pi_Swift requirement E20; adoption unit U3) on both paths, the Mac daemon and the iPhone's in-process engine. The change was reviewed twice and is committed (`71111a6`). A held step gets a Resume card in the same Store transaction (migration 14) with the audited `tool.interrupted` event; the user answers through `cards.respond`, and Memoshi then answers Pi. Run again is offered only while Pi's call is still open (a kill or crash), never after a Stop or a returned unknown outcome and never for an export, even one killed before its export record; the unknown-outcome case is narrower than D138's text and awaits the owner ([proposed decision](#proposed-decisions-2026-11-18)). When Run again is withdrawn, `card.updated` says so. The dispatch gate lets one Run again through at most once, and the model is told the user's choice even when the answer races Pi's commit. The Mac and iPhone show only the offered choices under the hold notice; the Inbox and notification surfaces are not built ([protocol.md](specs/protocol.md#current-step-settlement)). Since the 2026-11-18 fixes (`ec23475`): `chats.abort` records the Stop, so Run again stays withheld after a later kill; Run again of a call the policy asks about needs a new approval for that execution; and a step Pi lost after Memoshi recorded it as completed, or a replay-safe step whose tool is no longer registered as replay-safe, is held with a card instead of being settled ([deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)). This closes S6's "Stop leaves the Chat held" gap: after a Stop of `python_run`, Skip and Mark as done release the Chat. Verified in the iOS Simulator on 2026-11-15 on `e83854c`: only Skip and Mark as done, the card survives a relaunch, the Chat continues ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-15-memoshi-s6-stop-settle/README.md)); not on a device. The layout bug (the Skip button under the floating header) is fixed at the default text size: the header is a top safe-area bar at every size, capped at xxxLarge, and the live chat scrolls to the Resume card and hides the keyboard; the original test passed 10 of 10. A second fix for large text is kept: portrait passes at the default size, XL, XXXL and AX5, and landscape at the default size and XL. Two landscape cases were left open on 2026-11-15; the next two items give their state.
- **iPhone landscape at XXXL text.** On 2026-11-15 the header pill covered the approval card's "Allow once"; before the large-text fix the card was not found at all ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-15-memoshi-s6-stop-settle/README.md), `large-text/`). The landscape fix of 2026-11-18 (`9398f76`) keeps the header to one line, and in the iPhone 14 Pro Simulator (iOS 27.0) at default and XXXL text the header no longer covers Allow once ([first check](../../evidence/2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/landscape/README.md)). The transcript now brings the pending Card's buttons back into view, centred, after a rotation, after a Card arrives and after the keyboard hides: in the second check Allow once was clear of the header and the composer in every landscape shot at both sizes, with no drag, and at XXXL only its top 4 pt lay under the header bar's padding ([second check](../../evidence/2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/landscape-2/README.md)). **Still open:** the transcript gets only about 69 pt (default) and 45 pt (XXXL) in landscape, Deny (58 pt) does not fit at XXXL, and with the keyboard up no transcript shows and the tab bar, the Development Review row and the Palette buttons cover the composer; that needs a layout decision. Simulator only, no device; Resume choices on rotation and accessibility sizes not run.
- **Open: iPhone landscape at AX5 text** (its own item). The UI test cannot pick the model in the Model sheet, the same before the fix; it may be the test, not the app ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-15-memoshi-s6-stop-settle/README.md), `large-text/`). The 2026-11-18 landscape check did not run AX5.
- **S1 on `slice-2`.** The first S1 batches on the pin ran on commit `06d3803` with `qwen3:8b`, after Ollama was restarted with lighter settings (8,192-token context, q8_0 KV cache, one model: 5.63 GB) because the defaults pushed swap and free disk past the floor ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-14-memoshi-s1-slice2/README.md)). Kill batch: **50 of 50**, all five kill points 10 of 10. Approval batch: **9 of 10**; the failure is a model reasoning loop with no tool call, the runtime outcome was correct, and it was not rerun. 0 duplicate effects, 0 effects before a decision, every pending approval reattached. These are gate results from the development Mac, not the reference Macs; iOS has none. A second complete approval batch on 2026-11-15 reached **10 of 10** ([record](../../evidence/2026-11-14-memoshi-s1-slice2/real-approval-10-b/README.md)); the first batch's 9 of 10 stays on record (19 of 20 together). The criterion counts approvals and is silent on a second batch: per approval it is met (19 of 19 reattached), per batch it is not (the first batch failed, and phase-0 allows a rerun only after a harness defect). The gate rules; the reports count it as **fail** until then. The kill/resume row is open until a complete real-model batch runs the `subagent` point (which since 2026-11-18 checks the child and runs only when `--points` names it; the four development batches of 2026-11-19 are not one, [work of 2026-11-19](#work-2026-11-19)), the nested Code Mode and compaction points exist, and iOS runs ([S1 report](research/spikes/S1-runtime.md#3b-the-slice-2-batches-2026-11-14-qwen38b)). No decision yet records `qwen3:8b` as the kill-batch model ([proposed decision](#proposed-decisions-2026-11-18)).
- **Canonical CI after the 2026-11-14 work.** [canonical-ci-07](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-07/README.md) (`06d3803`) failed one fixed-wait test while another job pushed the load past 100, and [canonical-ci-08](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-08/README.md) (`07c3e42`) was stopped by its watcher under load. Both are kept as invalid runs, as are [canonical-ci-09](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-09/README.md) (`4658dcd`, stopped by its watcher under the same load) and [canonical-ci-10](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-10/README.md) (`4658dcd`, failed in step 3 because the native MLS artifacts were a link to a volume not mounted after a restart: machine setup, not code). The records of `4658dcd` were then made with a clean tree ([index](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/README.md)). CI: 17 of 17 steps, **1,305 Swift tests in 15 runs and 74 release-tooling tests** ([canonical-ci-11](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-11/README.md)). Full S2: passed with no failures; the load verifier passed, the D123 check 60 of 60 and the extra check `recovery.unacceptedOldEpochReported` 0; revocation refused at all five points (relay, authority, claim, renewal and link), with 0 grants and 0 writes after revocation; hand-over p50 57.28 ms with 8 devices and 169.69 ms with 54 ([s2-full-06](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-06/README.md)); they remain that commit's records, and `canonical-ci-06` and `s2-full-05` stay the records of `f22517d`. The records of `7df84f9`, with a clean tree: CI 17 of 17 steps, **1,305 Swift tests in 15 runs and 74 release-tooling tests** ([canonical-ci-12](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-12/README.md)); full S2 passed with no failures, the load verifier passed, the D123 check 60 of 60 and the extra check 0; revocation refused at all five points, with 0 grants and 0 writes after revocation; hand-over p50 265.00 ms with 8 devices and 253.24 ms with 54 ([s2-full-07](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-07/README.md)). `s2-full-07` ran the same binary as `s2-full-06`; the slowdown is put down to the host (about 10 GB of swap in use against 0, load 5–18) [INFERENCE, not measured]. **Caveat (2026-11-18):** each "60 of 60" here is the checker of that time; on `s2-full-07` it counts one always-true check and 13 checks that passed on counters missing from the report, and the fixed in-repo checker gives 38 of 76; the relay's revocation refusals were not tied to each probe. The one canonical run after `7df84f9` and before the fixes, [canonical-ci-13](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-13/README.md) on `754cca2` (2026-11-17), **failed** in package tests (21 issues, all in `BrowserNetworkBoundaryTests`, with VPN and Tailscale network extensions active) and did not reach the later packages. **The records of `a2ac274`** (the 2026-11-18 fixes and their documents) are the latest with the full S2 run, with a clean tree: [canonical-ci-15](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-15/README.md) passed all 17 steps with 1,465 Swift Testing tests in 15 runs plus 6 XCTest tests and 105 release-tooling tests (`canonical-ci-14` on the same commit is invalid, failed under another session's load); [s2-full-08](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/s2-full-08/README.md) passed with no failures, the fail-closed D123 checker 61 of 61, revocation refused at all five points with each refusal tied to its probe, 0 of 2,703 grants and 0 writes after revocation, and hand-over p50 56.31 ms with 8 devices and 209.17 ms with 54, close to `s2-full-06` ([deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)). The current canonical CI record is [canonical-ci-25](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-25/README.md) on `7f766c8` ([work of 2026-11-21](#work-2026-11-21)); canonical-ci-17 stays the record of `8e7bd5b` and canonical-ci-16 that of `6ef4d10` plus the XcodeGen change ([work of 2026-11-19](#work-2026-11-19)); `s2-full-08` stays the full S2 record.
- **Unchanged:** **Leave release blocked** and **Keep broader qualification blocked**; production tools stay off; no product feature starts before the Phase 0 gate.

<a id="d135-d137-owner-decisions-2026-11-14"></a>
**Owner decisions of 2026-11-14 ([D135](plan/decisions.md#d135)–[D137](plan/decisions.md#d137)):** the S3 and S4 owner decisions that the draft reports and the gate review listed as open are made.
- **S3.** The new-tab preconnect is a named exception, not a pass (D135); Apple and WebKit reports are to be filed, and their numbers go into the gate review. A signed-in bot's cross-site move uses a separate, throwaway profile (D136), now built, reviewed three times and committed (`fabd26e`). A site is the scheme plus the registrable domain from a bundled Public Suffix List (snapshot of 2026-11-13, MPL-2.0, in `NOTICE`). A return to the signed-in profile behaves like a normal cross-site GET (Lax cookies sent, Strict withheld), proven on the direct route and carried over to the gateway route; limits: the Referer of a return is always the leaving page's origin, and there is no return with JavaScript off. Final run `control-d136g-6c65b0d938cf` on owned fixtures passed every separate-profile check ([S3 §4](research/spikes/S3-webkit-browser.md#4-proposed-decision-updates), [evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-14-memoshi-s3-separate-profile/README.md)).
- **S4.** The 3 hard-link effects of the raw matrix and the writes through a file still open after export are accepted limits (D137), so the escape-suite row is open, no longer fail. The open-writer warning is built and committed (`71111a6`): the export tool's result carries `openWriters` data and a plain sentence that the model and the audit see; the Mac app has no export card yet, and the card shows the warning once it is built ([S4 §4](research/spikes/S4-this-mac-sandbox.md#4-proposed-decision-updates)). Multi-file non-atomic exports still need a ruling. Per-command denial attribution is also built and committed (`71111a6`, three review rounds): each command runs in its own tagged Seatbelt instance launched by the host, and denials, gaps and invocations go to the Store (migration 15). Limits: counts are lower bounds; a loss below 1,000 reports per second is seen only through probes; a later command's influence on an earlier command's child is not traceable; the open-writer check is best effort within 2 s and sees only the user's own processes ([S4 §4](research/spikes/S4-this-mac-sandbox.md#4-proposed-decision-updates), [evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-14-memoshi-s4-attribution/README.md)). Gate rows still need the reference box.
- **Local Network: no longer owed.** The full S3 no-bypass control ran on 2026-11-15 as `control-d136local-39e03b203c57`: 28/28, both `.local` positives reached their sinks, 0 product sink events, fail-closed 7/7, `strictPass` false only for the `_blank` preconnect (D135's named exception, still not a pass). No prompt appeared, because macOS attributed the request to the terminal app that launched the harness (`Tern.app`), which held the permission; so this run alone does not explain the earlier `.local` timeouts ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-14-memoshi-s3-separate-profile/README.md)).

<a id="work-2026-11-16"></a>
**Work of 2026-11-16** (committed in `419340d`, `59a4ff8`, `e37f5e0`, `d0782cd`, `ddefd02`, `92ff0df`, `2e67f65` and `754cca2`, with the records in `0ed7b5e`, `167ffb9`, `db4586c`, `f3a3ce4` and `3016ac1`; `2e67f65`, `3016ac1` and `754cca2` are not pushed, and the only canonical run on them, `canonical-ci-13`, failed, [above](#d134-pin-move-2026-11-14)). The reviews of that day are not retained: no `2026-11-16-*` evidence folder holds a review record, so "no blocker or high finding" and the review findings below rest on the commit messages and the fix tests in the tree. The 2026-11-18 deep review found a blocker and two highs in this work ([deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)).
- **H3 adopted (S4-T3).** The sandbox daemon's file tools now run under Pi_Swift `slice-2`'s guarded file service: private home and working copy read-write, toolchain roots read-only, a secret and host-exec policy taken from the profile's own D103 lists. H3's conformance suite passed (23 tests), Memoshi's guard tests 10 of 10 (one live confined-daemon round), the full MemoshiCore run exit 0; the raw matrix is unchanged (72/25/3/5) and attribution passed 3 of 3. The working-copy count of "42 tests including the held-descriptor case" is not in the evidence, whose README points at a session ledger; its `full-memoshicore-test.log` supports the 10 guard tests. The file-tools-unguarded defect is closed. Not run: Pi's complete environment conformance through the guarded daemon, and no reference box ([S4 report](research/spikes/S4-this-mac-sandbox.md), [evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-16-memoshi-s4-h3/README.md)).
- **S4 raw matrix in the repo (S4-T1).** The in-repo sources, compiled standalone with `swiftc` and a main shim, reproduced the 105-vector matrix (72/25/3/5); the `memoshi-qualification s4-matrix` subcommand itself did not run that day, and its default form failed. Since `2e67f65` it exits 2 when the only effects are the three accepted hard links and 3 for any other failure in the same run. The CLI first ran on 2026-11-18, after the fixes: 72/25/3/5 and exit 2 on the frozen and on the re-snapshotted profile ([run-1](../../evidence/2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/s4-matrix/run-1/README.md), [run-2](../../evidence/2026-11-18-memoshi-review-fixes/s4-matrix/run-2/README.md)). Alias establishment depends on the filesystem: HFS+ folds the U+200D joiner spellings to one inode, exFAT does not; U+200B on neither. `--aliases-volume` requires `--output-dir`.
- **Kill points (CH-01).** The chaos driver gained a `subagent` kill point (scripted model: 3 of 3, 2 of 2 with two kills per run, 6 of 6 over the six drivable points; [evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-16-memoshi-harness-items/)). Those runs never checked the child: the provider moved on to the effect even on an error result. Since the 2026-11-18 fixes the run checks the child, the child gets no tools, and `subagent` runs only when `--points` names it ([deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)); a complete real-model `subagent` batch is owed (the four of 2026-11-19 are development batches, [work of 2026-11-19](#work-2026-11-19)). `compaction` stays gated: Memoshi never starts a compaction step, and no crash-safety proof inside `pi.compaction` exists. Nested Code Mode waits for H1. Three S2 checkers (`check_d123_v2.py`, `check_d133_extra.py`, `verify_s2_samples.py`) and the runner `run-s2-full-v2.py` moved into `scripts/`; that they reproduce the `s2-full-07` output byte for byte is not recorded (the copied `check_d123_v2.py` is identical to the evidence copy).
- **S3 one-offs (T9).** `get-search` and `trusted-main-input` are fixed in the browser task runner (wait for the navigation commit, bounded 30 s; `staleSnapshot` retried inside waiting reads; a native type is checked against the live field value) and passed in 3 of 3 full runs (7/8 each; only `trusted-cross-origin-frame` fails). No unit test builds a real `BrowserSession`. T4, T6 and T7 stay open ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-16-memoshi-s3-tickets/README.md)).
- **S3 form gate and T10 (evening).** `92ff0df` added a form gate meant to be consulted first for destinations that are not granted. The 2026-11-18 review found it could never take effect: the throwaway profile copies the bot's grants exactly, and the network boundary refuses an ungranted origin (`originOutsideRoute`) before any form logic, so `92ff0df` changed no outcome. The gate is removed since the 2026-11-18 fixes; the navigation decision is one AppCore function with its own tests, and a granted cross-site form still moves to the throwaway profile with its staged permit or is refused ([deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)). The "0 blockers and 0 high" review of that evening has no record. T10's crash case was a relaunch unit test that duplicated `sweepsLeftovers` and exercised no crash (removed on 2026-11-18) and a real-kill sweep scenario in the control; the scenario's child now opens a live throwaway profile and is killed by SIGKILL. S3's 06 row 9 stays **open**: it needs its control run ([S3 report](research/spikes/S3-webkit-browser.md)). On 2026-11-19 the sweep ran and passed, and the form tasks passed on the direct route; row 9's gateway route still has no result ([work of 2026-11-19](#work-2026-11-19)).
- **S1.14, S1.15, S1.17 (evening; `qwen3:8b`, development Mac).** 20 aborts: 19 of 19 delivered aborts idled within 1 s, background children 10 of 10, 0 duplicates in 17 committed effects; the 6 strict-check failures are the model re-delegating after the abort. Long chat: both 30-turn runs 26 of 26 recalls, provisional (Memoshi starts no compaction step; final answer at M1). Combined contract suite 503–514 s, over the 5-minute bar; upstream 5 / 5 / 8 issues, from oracle-prerequisite refusals (4 `ProviderWirePersistenceTests`, 3 `StorageInteropTests`) and a reproducible `HarnessToolsTests` failure ([evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-16-memoshi-s1-runs/README.md)). None of these is a counted S1 threshold row, so the S1 counts do not change. On 2026-11-19 the combined suite took about 494 s serial and 285 s in three shards under load ([work of 2026-11-19](#work-2026-11-19)).
- **Resume outliers (CH-01).** The 7.7 s and 49.6 s resume samples of 2026-11-04 are one-off host stalls, not Memoshi code: on the same seeds today p95 42 ms and 166 ms; a new guard test bounds every product phase of the ready path at 2 s ([S1 §3c](research/spikes/S1-runtime.md#3c-the-ch-01-resume-outliers-2026-11-16), [evidence](../../evidence/2026-11-16-memoshi-resume-outliers/README.md)). Since 2026-11-18 that test fails when a phase's median, or more than one of the twelve ready totals, reaches 2 s; one slow reopen is printed, not failed, because a stalled host looks like that and the canonical rule never reruns a failure. It runs in process with the fixture provider, so it does not see process spawn or real model discovery. The S1 resume row becomes **open** (no reference-box Release series), no longer fail.
- **Review findings, fixed test-first** (the fix tests are in the tree; the review itself is not retained). Medium: the guard's secret policy disagreed with the profile on HFS+, where U+200D spellings fold to one inode (the guard allowed `.npmrc<U+200D>`, the profile denied it); not exploitable, because the daemon runs under the profile and the roots are on APFS. The policy then folded NFC, dropped default-ignorable code points and ASCII-lowercased; since 2026-11-18 the guard refuses a path when either the profile's own regular expressions, run on the path's bytes, or host admission's predicate would ([deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)). Low: credential-mask placeholders could override `HOME`, `TMPDIR` and the guard roots (host-set variables now win, and since 2026-11-18 a mask with a host-owned name is refused); the `chaos` default `--points` included the undrivable `compaction` (since 2026-11-18 the default is the five gate points); the resume guard test measured the crash-image copy inside "store open" (now recorded separately and unbounded).

**After the work of 2026-11-13** ([code review](#code-review-2026-11-13), [remaining items and evidence runs](#d131-work-2026-11-13), [limits closed](#d133-work-2026-11-13)):
- **Done:** the review's fixes, D123–D133, and the canonical CI run and the full S2 run on `4db72e2`, repeated on `f22517d` after D134, on `4658dcd` and on `7df84f9`; everything is pushed. The retained Python licence texts are bundled with the runtime (D133), as the release check requires ([D126](plan/decisions.md#d126)).
- **Open for the S2 report:** the slow hand-over is resolved by the group-state journal, but a group change still rewrites most of the native MLS state, about 1.4 MB at 54 devices; that is the remaining floor ([details](#d133-work-2026-11-13)). Not covered by any run here: physical iPhone and iPad, CloudKit against Apple's servers and a deployed relay; the loopback numbers are not deployed capacity; and the Mac and iOS app sources have no test target.
- **Fable review of all non-Fable work (user's instruction, 2026-11-14; still owed).** On 2026-11-13 the Fable 5.2 agents hit the provider's rate limit in the middle of the D133 round; the user chose to continue with Opus 5.5 (high) agents for that session only. HANDOFF gave the limit's end as **Wed 2026-11-18 at 08:00 (+03)** (the provider's retry-after on the last refused requests); the retired 2026-11-16 handoff said about 2026-11-20. When the user says Fable is back, the agent runs an independent Fable review. Commit messages do not record the model reliably (`92ff0df`, `ddefd02` and the 2026-11-16 records name none; the Cursor commits carry only a `Co-authored-by: Cursor` trailer), so the scope is an explicit range: **every commit in `cd440c4..8b9b170`, plus the documents commit of 2026-11-18 that follows `8b9b170`**, which includes the 2026-11-18 review's fix commits. The [deep review of 2026-11-18](#deep-review-2026-11-18) ran on Claude Opus 5.5 and does not replace this review unless the owner decides it does. Subagent model overrides are session settings, so a new session uses Fable by default. Review in these slices, each by one Fable reviewer with a shell, citing only what it ran:
  1. **Device layer:** `cd440c4`, `4db72e2`: the join of a new device into a busy group, the group state saved as a journal (crash safety, conversion of existing state), telling a removed device of its removal, and the hello sealed under the send gate.
  2. **Pin move to `slice-2`:** `de5e8ee`: the allow-all engine tool policy with Memoshi's own checks deciding every call, the automatic settle of a step Memoshi never dispatched, the rewritten model text, the durable dispatch record, and the clean-clone pin check.
  3. **Apps:** `f12a6ff`, `c36711a`: app logic moved into tested packages, removal erase, full group, the Python licence texts, and the Debug-only `python_run` tool.
  4. **Harnesses and runtime tests:** `58f2b01`, `01352bf`, `f94a877`: the S2 revocation proof and qualified size, the S4 export fix, the S1 chaos and S3 no-bypass controls.
  5. **Claims against evidence:** `3b503de`, `d0dc184`, `18b4c18`, `de579ef`, `f22517d`, `b8030d6` and decision D134: every number and pass or fail statement in the records, the six spike report drafts and the gate review draft must match the evidence folders it cites.
  6. **2026-11-14 to 2026-11-16:** `fabd26e` (D136), `71111a6` (D138 settlement, S4 attribution, D137 warning), `07c3e42`, `61ba644`, `0e821d4`, the 2026-11-16 work (`419340d`, `59a4ff8`, `e37f5e0`, `d0782cd`, `ddefd02`, `92ff0df`, `2e67f65`, `754cca2`) and every record and handoff commit in between.
  7. **2026-11-18 fixes:** `782caec`, `ec23475`, `3f9aa47`, `662ce10`, `89ca9f4`, `9398f76`, `8b9b170` and the documents commit after them, against the [deep review](#deep-review-2026-11-18)'s findings and its [proposed decisions](#proposed-decisions-2026-11-18).

  Findings go to fix agents on Fable, test-first, then the full check and new canonical records if code changes. Until this review is done, the Opus work is not counted as independently reviewed by Fable in the gate review.

<a id="session-2026-11-16"></a>
**Session facts of 2026-11-16 (from the retired `docs/HANDOFF_2026-11-16.md`).** That file (committed in `f3a3ce4`, partly corrected in `3016ac1`) is deleted on 2026-11-18, so this file is the one handoff. It contradicted the gate draft, the evidence and the code in several places: it counted S1 as marginal, named S1-T16, S1-T18 and "L12", which do not exist, gave a command for a missing `Packages/BrowserApp` and pointed at evidence folders that are not the gate-counted ones. Read the gate draft and the reports instead. Its facts that hold:
- **App smoke, not recorded.** The signed Mac app on disk was stale (2026-11-13) and its bundle broken. It was rebuilt with `scripts/build-apps.sh` (Mac and iPhone, Debug and Release) from the code of `92ff0df`: the file names `167ffb9` and also "before `167ffb9`", and `167ffb9` and `db4586c` change only documents. Launched, `memoshid` started with the app, Ollama at `127.0.0.1:11434` served `qwen3:8b`, and the app logged a `Cold chat` on that route. Nobody typed a message into the app. Only the retired file reports this; no evidence folder records it.
- **Model picker default.** The app's saved model choice was changed from `local/qwen3:0.6b` to `local/qwen3:8b` with `defaults write com.mehmetcanbudak.memoshi.mac` (the picker changes it back). On 2026-11-18, `defaults read com.mehmetcanbudak.memoshi.mac` shows `text-chat.selected-model = "local/qwen3:8b"`.
- **Deletions the user approved on 2026-11-16.** The raw relay journals of `s2-full-01` to `s2-full-05` (about 8 GB, written by `memoshi-qualification s2`, not by Pi_Swift); their READMEs, indexes, digests and checker outputs stay, and the `s2-full-04` README still describes its journal as present. The `errata-suite-live` (about 1.3 GB) and `errata-run-20261109` caches. `~/MemoshiBuildRoot`, recreated since: `canonical-ci-13` used it on 2026-11-17 ([run.json](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/canonical-ci-13/run.json)).
- **`qwen3:0.6b` is not used for new kill batches.** On 2026-11-14, on the old candidate, it failed 19 of 60 real-model runs: 16 empty final answers and 3 skipped tool calls, all model behaviour ([kill batch](../../evidence/2026-11-14-memoshi-s1-chaos/real-50/README.md), [approval batch](../../evidence/2026-11-14-memoshi-s1-chaos/real-approval-10/README.md)). The gate-counted batches use `qwen3:8b`; recording that is a [proposed decision](#proposed-decisions-2026-11-18).
- **`STATUS_REPORT_2026-11-13.md` is not in the repository.** A copy is in the [code-review record](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-code-review/) as `STATUS_REPORT_2026-11-13-claude-code-session.md`. Do not commit it unless the user asks; whether to keep or archive it is the user's call.
- **Process hazards.**
  - One build or test run at a time: concurrent SwiftPM and Xcode builds replace each other's products and skew timings. The 2026-11-18 agents held one lock (`lockf /tmp/memoshi-review/build.lock`) for every build, test and harness run.
  - `MEMOSHI_BUILD_ROOT`: canonical runs set it (`canonical-ci-12` and `canonical-ci-13` used `~/MemoshiBuildRoot`); the package test runs of 2026-11-16 and 2026-11-18 unset it and built on the internal disk. It is not mandatory either way.
  - Before a timing-sensitive run, check that no `yes` process runs and the load is low; another session's load script stopped `canonical-ci-08` and `canonical-ci-09`, and the canonical runner's watch rule now records both ([index](../../evidence/2026-11-13-memoshi-canonical-s2/README.md)).
  - New evidence goes in `../evidence/<YYYY-MM-DD>-memoshi-<name>/`, never in the repository.
  - Agents can fail at spawn when the model provider's account is out of credit (2026-11-16: OpenRouter HTTP 402 for the GLM agents). The Opus 5.5 and GLM model override of that day was for the session only and is not saved.
  - Commits are logical, after the full check passes. `2e67f65` and `754cca2` went in without one (`canonical-ci-13` failed), and nothing after `f3a3ce4` is pushed.

<a id="proposed-decisions-2026-11-18"></a>
**Proposed decisions awaiting the owner (2026-11-18).** The deep review and its fixes rest on readings that no decision records yet. None of these is in [`decisions.md`](plan/decisions.md); each needs the owner's yes, a change, or a no, and would go in as a new decision. Where the code already behaves one way, the item says so, so that a no means a code change. Everything still open before the Phase 0 gate and Phase 1, these decisions and the owner's other inputs included, is collected in [`plan/remaining-work.md`](plan/remaining-work.md).
1. **Run again after an unknown returned outcome (narrows [D138](plan/decisions.md#d138)).** D138 says "Other interrupted steps offer all three choices under D51's rules"; the code (`71111a6`) also withholds Run again from a step held because its execution returned an unknown outcome ([protocol.md](specs/protocol.md#current-step-settlement)). Proposed: "A step held because its execution returned an unknown outcome offers only Skip and Mark as done. Run again is offered only while the engine's call is still open after a kill or crash, and never after a Stop or for an export (D138)."
2. **Run again needs a new approval (a reading of [D51](plan/decisions.md#d51) and [D52](plan/decisions.md#d52)).** In the code since `ec23475`. Proposed: "Run again of a replay-unsafe call is admitted again under the policy and permission mode in force when the user answers the Resume card. Where that policy asks, a new approval card is shown for that execution; if it is denied or expires, the call does not run again. A replay-safe step reuses its original approval."
3. **The inviter's pairing outcome (replaces part of [D131](plan/decisions.md#d131)).** D131 says that if the group changed before the last pairing message was sent, "the pairing is refused and started again"; that cannot work once the add is accepted, because the joiner is already a member. In the code since `662ce10` ([protocol.md §12.15](specs/protocol.md#1215-ways-out-of-the-two-device-holds)). Proposed: "Once the authority has accepted an add, the pairing has succeeded on the inviter: when the inviter's host has completed it, the inviter sends the joiner's final hello on the pairing link when that hello's seal, or the same hello sealed again in a later group state because its own state closed first, was accepted, and otherwise ends the link; when the host's completion of the accepted add fails, the inviter reports the pairing as pending, not failed, ends the link, and the node completes the add on its retry and then delivers the hello; the joiner completes its join from that hello on the link or from the group's feed, and the inviter reports failure only when the add was not committed."
4. **Where the device transport store lives (extends [D133](plan/decisions.md#d133)'s erase list).** In the code since `662ce10`; no app constructs the store yet. Proposed: "The device transport store (`transport.jsonl`) lives in `<zone>/transport`, the directory `CloudKitDeviceHost.transportStoreDirectory()` names, and is erased with the other stores after a verified removal."
5. **Commands run in one host-launched `sandbox-exec` each (amends [D77](plan/decisions.md#d77) and [D96](plan/decisions.md#d96)).** D77 and D96 say commands run inside the sandboxed PiEnv daemon through `RemoteExecutionEnv`; since `71111a6` each command is started by the unconfined host through PiEnv's `LocalExecutionEnv` in its own tagged Seatbelt instance, which per-command denial attribution needs. Proposed: "Each This Mac command runs in its own Seatbelt instance, tagged for denial attribution and started by the host through PiEnv's `LocalExecutionEnv`. The host only spawns it: it never looks up the command's working directory, which the confined shell enters. Closing a session ends the commands' process groups and waits at most 10 seconds; a child that left those groups is a recorded limit."
6. **The kill-batch model.** Proposed: "S1's real-model kill and approval batches use `qwen3:8b` on Ollama (8,192-token context, q8_0 KV cache) on the development Mac; `qwen3:0.6b` is not used for gate batches." Cloud-model kill batches still need their own go-ahead.
7. **E21: Code Mode's discovery globals.** `searchTools`, `describeTool` and `describeNamespace` read declared metadata and pass no policy hook ([E21 note](research/2026-11-16-e21-discovery-tools-policy.md)). Choose one. (a) Proposed default: "Code Mode's discovery globals read only declared tool metadata and are not tool calls; the tool hook decides every call made with what they return." (b) Ask Pi_Swift for a hook that lets Memoshi's policy decide each discovery call; that is a Pi_Swift API change outside the `slice-2` pin.
8. **Reads that write events.** `chats.history`, `tasks.graph` and `tasks.history` open a conversation, which can commit lease-claim audit events and `ensureBotPolicy` events, and `tasks.graph` reconciles outcomes; their results do not wait for those events ([protocol.md §4](specs/protocol.md#4-conventions)). Choose one. (a) Proposed default: "A read's result does not wait for events its own conversation open committed; those events arrive on the event stream like any other." (b) Reads follow the events-before-response rule like mutating methods, at the cost of waiting out any replay in progress.
9. **`tool.interrupted` for Memoshi's not-dispatched settlement.** When a replay-unsafe step was cut off before Memoshi's dispatch record, Memoshi settles it as Skip in its own name (`not_dispatched`) and emits no `tool.interrupted`, only `tool.completed` with `ok: false` and `errorCode: interrupted`, because the event's `effect` values (`rerun`, `outcome_check`, `resume_card`) do not fit ([events.md §5.6](specs/events.md#56-tool-and-code-mode)). Choose one. (a) Proposed default: "Memoshi's own settlement of a step that was never dispatched emits no `tool.interrupted`; the step's tool result tells the model it did not run." (b) Add an `effect` value `not_dispatched` to the protocol and emit the event.
10. **`git init` inside the This Mac sandbox.** Older than the range, measured on 2026-11-18: `git init sub` fails on `.git/hooks/` and `git init --template= sub` fails on `.git/config`, because [D103](plan/decisions.md#d103) classes both as host-exec paths that the profile keeps unwritable. A coding bot cannot create a repository in its working copy, and `git clone` fails the same way [INFERENCE: it writes `.git/config` too; not run]. No default is proposed: letting the bot write `.git/config` in its own repositories reopens what D103 closed, and the alternative is a host-side git for creating repositories ([S4 report](research/spikes/S4-this-mac-sandbox.md)).
11. **Pairing a removed device again.** [protocol.md §12.5](specs/protocol.md#125-revocation) says a removed device "pairs again as a new device", but the same section's list of what the hosts keep after a verified removal includes the device's Keychain identity. With the identity kept, creating a new identity fails as a duplicate, and the removal of an unenrolled identity refuses an enrolled one, so the lab has no way back. Not built. Choose one. (a) Proposed default: "After a verified removal, the host removes the removed device's enrolled identity and its files, so the device can pair again as a new device with a new identity; a removed identity is never added again." This needs a Keychain call that removes an enrolled identity only after a verified removal, in `DeviceEnrollmentKeychain` and the apps' `LabKeychain`. (b) Keep the identity, and record that a removed device cannot pair again until its app data is reset.

**Execution hold (2026-10-23; changed 2026-11-03):** the user authorized documentation reconciliation, verification and separate commits, followed by a report. On 2026-11-03 the user delegated the hold ("do what's best"): **deliberate kill runs on the Mac are released** against isolated synthetic homes, owned processes and local or controlled models. Physical iPhone/iPad runs are released in principle but need the in-app engine host and device provisioning first, and cloud-model kill batches need a separate go-ahead because they spend the user's allowance ([phase-0 §3](plan/phase-0.md#team-and-calendar)). Before a `slice-N` tag every such run is development evidence ([D90](plan/decisions.md#d90)); since [D134](plan/decisions.md#d134) (2026-11-14) the pin is the tag `slice-2`, so S1 runs from then on count. The steps below are planned work, not a record of completed runs.

**Former hold scope (2026-10-23, left to the recommendation):** the hold covered runs that produce Phase 0 gate evidence on the iPhone or iPad, or by deliberately killing processes: the kill cases of S1.1, S1.7, S1.8, S1.11 and S1.13, with P0-15's chaos runs; S1.18 and Pi_Swift's Thu 11-12 iPhone measurement (its M5), with its M6 on-device runs; S2 between devices; S3's iOS check; S5's installs on the iPhone and iPad; and S6. It never covered builds, CI or focused tests. The kill cases in P0-05 and P0-06 needed the release by Mon 2026-11-02 (it came on 11-03), and the device and chaos runs need it by Mon 2026-11-09 [INFERENCE: from [phase-0 §3](plan/phase-0.md#team-and-calendar)]; a later start moves the Phase 0 gate unless the [cut list](plan/phase-0.md#cut-list) absorbs it.

**The user's decisions of 2026-11-03:**
- **Commit and push: done.** The working tree went into nine logical commits, `d4793c0` (protocol) through `62866f6` (docs), pushed to `origin/main`. Remote CI now stops in seconds at `scripts/check-prerequisites.sh` by design: the runner workspace has no copy of the unpublished Pi_Swift candidate, and the disk has less than the 15 GB a clean CI workspace needs ([README](../README.md#build-and-test)). Local `scripts/ci.sh` is unaffected.
- **Disk: left as is** (about 8 GB free). Agents reuse existing build directories and remove only their own temporary files.
- **Release block: unchanged.** **Leave release blocked** (the GitHub artifact-quota hold) and **Keep broader qualification blocked** stand, so no `slice-N` existed then and S1 results remained development evidence. (Pi_Swift tagged `slice-1` and `slice-2` on 2026-11-12; [D134](plan/decisions.md#d134) moves the pin to `slice-2`.)
- **Execution hold: delegated** and changed as above.
- **Code Mode limits: delegated**, accepted as proposed by [D98](plan/decisions.md#d98) ahead of Mon 2026-11-09.
- **Developer ID, App IDs and profiles: last.** P0-16's signing items, S5 (P0-14) and device provisioning wait for them.
- **Public-files domain: development host live.** Per the [D99 amendment](plan/decisions.md#d99) (2026-11-10), sign-in development uses `memoshi-auth.pages.dev`; Apple's CDN serves its association file. The user buys a domain before release and attaches it to the same Cloudflare Pages project; test sign-ins are then redone.
- **Reference Macs and the clean macOS 27 VM: last.** The VM needs a restore image and disk far larger than the free space, so nothing was downloaded.

**Earlier dated actions (2026-10-23):**
- **Signing setup:** reuse Apollo's existing developer team if active and authorized; its retained TestFlight/WeatherKit distribution evidence was inspected on 2026-10-23, not its live renewal status. No new membership purchase is assumed. P0-16 still configures Memoshi's own identifiers/profiles, and P0-14 proves its G2 signing/notarization path.
- **In W1:** P0-22 records licence/provenance and unresolved future-release rights; it is not a paid-lawyer prerequisite to P0-19 ([D91](plan/decisions.md#d91)). No designer booking or payment action is required ([D93](plan/decisions.md#d93)). Done on 2026-10-23: the private GitHub repository `mehmetcanbudak/Memoshi`, with its self-hosted runner on the development Mac (P0-01, [D90](plan/decisions.md#d90)). P0-22 recorded on 2026-11-03 ([review](research/2026-11-03-provenance-and-licence-review.md)).
- **By W2 (Mon 2026-11-02):** the Macs Phase 0 measures on: the base 16 GB M1 reference box and an 8 GB Mac ([phase-0 §5](plan/phase-0.md#spikes)); the clean macOS 27 VM by W4. Deferred to last on 2026-11-03, above.

1. **Continue from implemented chat, policy and review, not another skeleton.** P0-01 to P0-03 landed early; the [foundation](#foundation-implementation-proof), [chat](#text-chat-development-proof), [policy/approvals](#policy-approval-development-proof), [committed outcomes](#committed-tool-outcome-proof), [native folder management](#folder-management-development-proof) and [runtime copy/export integration](#runtime-working-copy-development-proof) distinguish actual results from open gates. Preserve real chat state, separately labelled design examples, current review homes and unrelated prototype data. `scripts/ci.sh` remains the full pre-push check. Phase 0's Friday 2026-11-27 gate is unchanged; after it, [D122](plan/decisions.md#d122)'s estimated calendar replaces D87's.
2. **Qualify remaining runtime/policy/sandbox and broker contracts before enabling tools (P0-05/P0-06/P0-20/P0-12).** Build against Pi_Swift `slice-2` as pinned ([D134](plan/decisions.md#d134)) and preserve **Leave release blocked** and **Keep broader qualification blocked**: no quota/account/billing workaround. S1 results count from this pin on ([D90](plan/decisions.md#d90)); those recorded on the old candidate `892585fc` stay development evidence. The engine's interrupted-step settlement (`harness.settle`, E20; adoption unit U3) is built and committed (`71111a6`) and makes Run again, Skip and Mark as done available. Continue from the [current conversation/native/runtime proofs](#multi-conversation-device-proof), not another scaffold; preserve real cloud/Stop/normal-reopen behavior while qualifying remaining credential lock/iCloud and runtime cases. `slice-2` carries the [H1/H2 wait, interrupted/nested-result settlement and fence contracts and the H3 guarded file-service initializer](research/spikes/2026-11-02-pi-swift-consumer-handoff.md) (`EnvDaemonSession.init(transport:files:)`); Memoshi adopts them before product integration. H4's workload and limits are accepted ([D98](plan/decisions.md#d98)); cross-backend/device measurements remain. Full metadata-preserving workflows, product PiEnv/tool composition, hard duration/global/live-writer quotas, a working confined gateway, privacy/performance, replicated grants and standing/high-risk/recovery workflows remain. Tools stay off. The iOS host now exists; physical-device cases still need provisioning and real-device qualification. D89's differential/reference gates, no JavaScriptCore and D46 fallbacks remain unchanged.
3. **Finish S2 from the Mac-local device/relay prototype, not from zero:** retain trusted enrollment, signed group transitions, authority-backed fencing and digest/catch-up checks while qualifying pairing, direct/relay links, hand-over and [S2.12](plan/phase-0.md#s2-12)'s raw active/idle claim-renewal measurements. CloudKit, MLS, durable authority/replica storage, physical devices and deployed relay load remain distinct open work. Authority loss, missing plaintext and unresolved reservations must remain fail-closed. O10 stays open beyond the exercised synthetic loads; the initial 3-day timebox is not a claim that the expanded scope fits.
4. **Run the remaining S3 to S6 gates:** WebKit on physical Mac/iOS references; complete D96 private-copy This Mac qualification with S4's unchanged daemon/guard/proxy/mask/escape thresholds; and S5's separate provisioning/CloudKit/push, G2 Developer ID, notarization and internal TestFlight build ([D56](plan/decisions.md#d56), [D58](plan/decisions.md#d58)). S6 (P0-23) remains the week-4 one-builder-day Pyodide cold-start/memory/background measurement on iPhone 14 Pro ([D80](plan/decisions.md#d80)); it is first on the Phase 0 cut list, moving to W52 if cut ([D122](plan/decisions.md#d122)). Its fallback order remains trimmed/lazy Pyodide, then Mac Python for phones, then native CPython only if both fail ([D79](plan/decisions.md#d79)). No calendar change follows from D96. The [S3 draft](research/spikes/2026-11-03-s3-webkit-browser-draft.md) distinguishes the exercised visible one-pixel host from detached hidden-page 1 Hz throttling; credential/memory/native evidence is not a complete S3 pass. [S4](research/spikes/2026-11-03-s4-this-mac-sandbox-draft.md) still has no passing gate row; a safe functional confined gateway and Pi_Swift H3 seam remain blockers.
5. **Review the implemented interface against references and record the reuse findings (P0-19).** Since [D106](plan/decisions.md#d106), P0-19's lead reference is Cognitio's design, rebuilt in Memoshi's own code: Cognitio leads the shell on Mac, iPad and iPhone, and Viticci's grammar stays for native Apple-data cards, Apps and phone navigation. Read [Direction from 2026-11-13](specs/design-reference.md#direction-2026-11-13) in [`docs/specs/design-reference.md`](specs/design-reference.md); the record's earlier sections, with the Current/Warm comparison and the original avatar/gallery development work, keep the evidence of the direction [D92](plan/decisions.md#d92) and [D94](plan/decisions.md#d94) set, and that preview is frozen at git tag `design-preview-2026-11-13`. Builders read Cognitio's running app and source as a behaviour reference and may still inspect Kuvo references under [D91](plan/decisions.md#d91) and record verified values, but copy no Kuvo- or Cognitio-derived source file, asset or string, so the reuse D91 permits is not taken for the interface. P0-22 records terms and unresolved redistribution rights, not a prerequisite. Unchecked tokens stay provisional; native UI/accessibility observations do not supply reference-device performance or final visual approval. Keep private captures outside the repo. D93 requires no designer hire.
6. **Install on your own devices** ([D67](plan/decisions.md#d67)). v1 is for the user's own Mac, iPhone and iPad, with no App Store. The Mac app stays Developer ID-signed and notarized. The iPhone and iPad app goes out as internal TestFlight builds, which skip Beta App Review and expire after 90 days, so CI uploads a fresh build in time; an Xcode development build lasts up to a year [Claim: 2026 guides quoting Apple]. A public release is O13, decided after v1.0.
7. **Plan the two later probes,** 3 days each with [INFERENCE] pass marks, measured on the reference devices, the user's iPhone 14 Pro and iPad Pro 12.9-inch (M1) ([D80](plan/decisions.md#d80)): the Swift engine probe opens Phase 2 in W52 ([04 §4.17](plan/04-architecture.md#swift-engine-probe), [D68](plan/decisions.md#d68)), and the iSH probe opens Phase 3 in W135 ([06 §12](plan/06-computers-and-browsers.md#ish-probe), [D69](plan/decisions.md#d69)); both weeks are [D122](plan/decisions.md#d122)'s estimates. Their fallbacks are in [10-roadmap §13](plan/10-roadmap.md#13-what-could-change-the-plan).
8. **Buy the release domain before release** ([D80](plan/decisions.md#d80), [D99](plan/decisions.md#d99) as amended 2026-11-10): sign-in development already runs on `memoshi-auth.pages.dev`. Before a release build, the user buys the domain and attaches it to the `memoshi-auth` project for the MCP client document and the universal-link file; the Mac update feed, signed model catalog and website also need it. Nothing that embeds `pages.dev` ships in a release.
9. **Housekeeping:**
   - This folder and `~/Documents/memoshi/Pi_Swift` became git repositories on 2026-10-22. Kuvo source material was removed from this one before its first commit, so none of it is in git history ([D55](plan/decisions.md#d55)). The evidence folders stay outside both.
   - `~/Documents/memoshi/boop/desktop/` (an untracked 15.5k-line Rust port) and about 30 uncommitted Convex skill folders in boop exist only on this disk.
   - The dated 2026-11-03 free-space observation was about 8 GB. The current clean-workspace prerequisite is 15 GB free on the checkout volume ([README](../README.md#build-and-test)). External build scratch does not waive that floor. Agents remove only their own temporary files, not unrelated user data.
   - `~/Documents/Memoshi-Plan` is an active prototype with its own agent ([D65](plan/decisions.md#d65)). The user passes that agent the three rewordings to "Node Pi until Pi_Swift is slice-ready" ([D90](plan/decisions.md#d90)): its `MASTER_PLAN.md` line "Do not embed Pi in Swift", `docs/02-architecture.md`'s "Embed Pi in Swift: No", and the 1.3.0 pin in `sidecar/package.json`. Memoshi's agents do not edit that repository; Memoshi ports only proven pieces from it, under [D33](plan/decisions.md#d33) and [D36](plan/decisions.md#d36).

The three evidence folders sit outside both repositories (Memoshi and Pi_Swift) and are never committed: they hold third-party material. Each has a `MANIFEST.tsv` of SHA-256 hashes, and `~/Documents/memoshi/evidence/README.md` indexes them. Never copy their contents into this repo.

**Licence rule ([D33](plan/decisions.md#d33), [D69](plan/decisions.md#d69), [D91](plan/decisions.md#d91)):** normally copy permissively licensed code with attribution. Explicit exceptions are the GPL-3.0 iSH/Open Minis module, linked only by the iPhone/iPad app, and qualifying personal/noncommercial Kuvo Code reuse with its original terms and notices, which [D106](plan/decisions.md#d106) does not take for the interface. Kuvo Box, OpenBot, Lorca, multica and Grok Bot remain ideas-only sources. There is no blanket permission to copy artwork or to redistribute incompatible combined works.

**Kuvo source/reuse ([D91](plan/decisions.md#d91)):** the user and agents may inspect GitHub source and copy/adapt code for the personal-use project. The former clean-room/source-context rules are superseded. Record revision/path provenance and required notices before an import is consumed; do not call retained Kuvo material Apache-2.0. No fork or replacement frontend is selected, original branding remains, and private captures stay outside both repositories.

**Interface code ([D106](plan/decisions.md#d106)):** none of that reuse is taken for the interface. The shell is written new in Memoshi as one SwiftUI codebase; no Kuvo- or Cognitio-derived source file, asset or string enters this repository, and code is not translated file by file. SwiftTerm, Textual, WhisperKit and the usage-meter library that UsageKit forks stay usable from their original upstreams, not from Cognitio's forks. O13 stays open: nothing built under D106 may rule out a public or paid release.
